On molecules, however, with distinguishable atoms and global attention, neither limit prevents sinks; what stops them lies in the model. We find that the attention scoring function is the main cause. Replacing GRIT's gated logit with a dot product raises its sink rate from 0.09 to 0.68 on identical graphs. In Graph Transformers using dot-product attention, removing the sink gives about the same error as removing a random node, while GRIT's most-attended nodes matter to its predictions. Attention sinks are therefore a property of dot-product attention rather than of Transformers. In our experiments, they form when dot-product scoring has a distinguishable node to concentrate on.


### [50] [Counterfactual Gate-Support Decomposition: Separating Selection from Redistribution in Training-Data Filtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19343)
*Hyojung Han, Jongmin Kim, Seung-Hun Jeon*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: A training-data quality gate changes both which examples survive within regions of the input space and how training mass is distributed across those regions, so a gate-versus-random comparison does not isolate these mechanisms. We introduce Counterfactual Gate-Support Decomposition (CGSD), a four-arm, budget-matched design that crosses gate eligibility with support distribution and decomposes the observed gate effect into within-support selection, between-support redistribution, and their interaction. The practical constraint is finite-pool realizability: we characterize mass and token-assembly conditions under which the required arms can be constructed, together with a support-extinction case in which the ordinary design is undefined. On Korean-English and Korean-Japanese retrieval, the mean within-support selection component is the only one that exceeds its calibrated null band (+29.7 and +5.6 holdout items; 30/30 positive replicates), decisively in Korean-English and marginally in Korean-Japanese; redistribution and interaction remain unresolved at the available power. The same pattern holds for a second, unrelated gate family -- a model-confidence threshold rather than a deterministic verifier -- judged against its own null (+27.3 items, 9/9 positive), so the result is not a property of one gate's mechanism. On a mathematics gate, zero-eligibility supports leave the ordinary design undefined at every budget, while the extended design is constructible only inside narrow budget bands that an exhaustive scan locates and that the natural operating point misses -- the assembly condition separating a property of the pool from a limitation of the packer. CGSD therefore returns a decomposition, a constructible-but-underpowered design, or an explicit non-constructibility result, and says which; it prevents support redistribution from being silently interpreted as sample-level gate quality.


### [51] [Integrated Novelty Detection Systems: From Sensor Networks to Multi-Novelty Computer Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19347)
*Irené Tematelewo*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Novelty detection is crucial in various technological and scientific domains. Its importance spans from ensuring the reliability of sensor networks to enhancing the adaptability of computer vision systems. As AI systems become increasingly sophisticated and complex, effective anomaly detection is essential for maintaining reliability and accuracy. This work addresses novelty detection in weather sensor networks and computer vision. In weather sensor networks, particularly for monitoring rainfall, detecting blocked rain gauges can significantly impact data reliability, food security, and disaster management. This work evaluates four existing quality control methods for detecting blocked rain gauges and demonstrates their limitations, particularly in detecting clusters of faulty sensors. By comparing methods such as the Conditional Mixture Model (CMM), PSWQ (Fz-filter), Kernel Regression, and Gaussian Method, we identify their shortcomings and propose three novel quality control techniques that integrate probabilistic fault detection and consistency-based diagnosis framework to improve timely detection of blocked sensors, while reducing false alarms. In the realm of computer vision, current systems often fail to handle multiple types of novelty due to their focus on single-novelty scenarios. We highlight the need for integrated systems capable of handling multiple types of novelty. To support this, we introduce the Novel Snapshot Serengeti (NOVEL-SS) dataset, derived from the Snapshot Serengeti camera trap data, which includes various forms of novelty. We also present the DCA (Detect, Characterize, and Accommodate) baseline system, designed to handle multiple novelty by detecting, characterizing, and accommodating them. This work aims to advance the robustness and adaptability of novelty detection systems across both weather sensor networks and computer vision applications.


### [52] [Where to Start, How to Move: Analyzing Curricula under GRPO for Long-Horizon LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19349)
*Minchan Kim, Sungho Bae, Youngjin Kim, Hyunwoo Park*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) provides no reward-relative learning signal when all rollouts receive identical rewards. In long-horizon tasks that require multiple interactions, rewards are given only upon successful task completion, making all-fail groups common early in training and limiting opportunities to practice later steps. Guide Assisted GRPO replays a successful reference prefix before the learner continues. We study how initial positions, handoff units, and movement rules affect unassisted task completion. Across three model sizes on BFCL, starting near completion is more effective under a fixed turn-level threshold rule. Finer handoffs alone do not consistently help, but outcome-informed tool-call selection outperforms GRPO on trained and untrained task categories. Guided starts also improve AppWorld success on both evaluation splits. These findings highlight where learning starts and how starting positions move as key curriculum choices.


### [53] [BFHD: Bidirectional Feature Harmonization Decomposition for Heterogeneous Clinical Assessments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19352)
*Yuanhao Zhuo, Zixi Qin, Ling Qin, Wanqing Li*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Clinical assessments are often collected using heterogeneous assessment systems across centers and time, leading to records that mix different but related sets of measurements. This motivates harmonization beyond total-score linking. We formulate clinical harmonization at the measurement level as a bidirectional recoverability problem: given paired observations from two assessment systems, the goal is to identify which measurements can be reliably translated in both directions within an application-defined tolerance, while separating non-translatable components. We propose Bidirectional Feature Harmonization Decomposition (BFHD), a feasibility-driven framework that enforces bidirectionally coupled translation and uses feature-wise output gating to produce an explicit decomposition in the original measurement space. Experiments on synthetic data and real clinical assessment pairs show that BFHD achieves broader feasible harmonization coverage and improved subset stability compared to baselines.


### [54] [Asymptotic Behavior of Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19358)
*Katy Blumer, Da-Cheng Juan, Jon Kleinberg, Ravi Kumar, Cyrus Rashtchian, Andrew Tomkins*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: In this paper we study the behavior of transformer networks generating text over long periods of time. We show that even a three-token, single-layer attention-only transformer can fail to converge. We then study a rich class of transformers with arbitrary finite depth, layer normalization, MLPs, RoPE encodings, and an arbitrary mix of local and global layers. Every transformer in this class has a softmax temperature threshold above which its empirical cache distribution converges to a unique limit, independent of initial conditions. We show further that, while each transformer has a temperature threshold, the supremum of this set is infinite, so there is no global minimal temperature that forces all transformers to converge.


### [55] [Dynamic Graph World Modelling: Learning Future Dynamics from Temporal Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19364)
*Bo Li, Yue Yang, Yujin Huang, Siao Liu, David Lemphers, Sara E Rodriguez Gomez, Luke Borgnolo, Can Wang, Xin Zheng*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Dynamic graph learning models how entities and their relations evolve from historical observations to predict future graph states. Existing methods do not fully address dynamic graph world modelling, which requires learning transitions from temporal context to future latent graph states. This is difficult for three reasons: nodes and relations rely on different historical evidence, structural evolution is path-dependent, and future snapshots provide sparse and realization-specific targets. We propose Dynamic Graph World Modelling (DyG-WM), the first framework to jointly predict future node and relation representations from temporal context by answering three questions: what has evolved, how it evolved, and what will evolve next. To capture what has evolved, a historical node-relation context encoder summarizes node-neighborhood trajectories and pair-specific histories. To characterize how it evolved, a node-relation path-signature encoder captures ordered structural changes. To predict what will evolve next, a dual-level joint-embedding objective predicts target-snapshot node and relation representations without reconstructing the raw graph. Experiments on 12 link-prediction and two node-classification datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of DyG-WM across both tasks.


### [56] [Policy-Verified Self-Training: Learning Agent Compliance from Verifiable Policy Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19372)
*Min-Young Yu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Runtime policy verifiers can prevent agents from executing disallowed actions, but their decisions are typically used only for enforcement and discarded afterward. We ask whether these decisions can instead become verifiable policy rewards for training the agents they guard. We introduce Policy-Verified Self-Training (PVST), a post-training paradigm in which an agent generates its own interaction trajectories and an executable policy verifier, here a deterministic gate compiled from a written policy, evaluates whether each action complies. Compliant trajectories are reinforced, and violations become negatives for preference optimization; beyond the gate and the benchmark's task-success signal, PVST needs no stronger teacher model, no human preference labels and no attack-specific supervision, although rollouts are collected in the benchmark's attacked environment. On held-out AgentDojo tasks under prompt injection, policy-verified preference training of Qwen3-8B reduces violating trajectories from 37.2% to 10.3 $\pm$ 0.9% (three seeds) and successful attacks from 12.4% to 0.5% at equal or higher pooled task utility, although utility falls on travel, compared with 22.0% when negatives are selected by an attack oracle and 31.9 $\pm$ 0.9% with negatives that contain no violation. The learned behavior holds under an attack template never used in training (37.7% to 11.0%) and on a suite excluded from training (34.8% to 16.7%), and the retained verifier's intervention rate falls from 39.2% to 8.8% (seed 0). Our analysis identifies two conditions under which the verdict is most informative: success must not already imply compliance, and guarded actions must appear in both permitted and forbidden contexts.


### [57] [One-Step Prediction Fidelity Is a Poor Proxy for Planning Performance in JEPA World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19375)
*Joshua Cheung, Zhang He*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Latent world models are routinely selected, checkpointed and ablated on one-step prediction loss, on the assumption that a model which predicts the next latent better is a better model. In model-based RL this assumption is known to be unreliable; we ask whether it also fails for latent world models, where the training target is the model's own embedding and the loss is not expressed in interpretable units. We introduce a frozen-decoder protocol reporting one-step error in physical units, decomposed into the part already present in the encoder's representation and the part the predictor adds. Applying it to a recent end-to-end JEPA world model across three continuous-control environments, we move the metric by fine-tuning and measure goal-conditioned planning on the same checkpoints, with three training seeds and paired planning seeds throughout. Within one environment, a per-epoch ladder shows the relationship saturating after a single epoch: the first epoch improves fidelity by 32% and planning by 4.4 pp, but across the fifteen checkpoints that follow -- a further 15% fidelity improvement, monotone in every training seed -- the correlation between one-step error and planning success is r = -0.010. This is not a task-ceiling artifact: stratifying by a model-independent measure of training-data support, planning in the lowest-support stratum starts at 80%, leaving ample headroom, and is nonetheless flat from epoch 1 onward. Across environments the exchange rate is equally unstable: a 93% fidelity reduction on TwoRoom yields -0.7 pp of planning, while a 40% reduction on PushT yields +4.9 pp [+1.1, +8.9]. All results replicate under a decoder-free measure, latent drift. We also show that a matched continued-training control absorbs the entire fidelity gain we had attributed to an auxiliary objective.


### [58] [Robust Explanatory Analysis for Structured Optimization Networks (REASON) in Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19378)
*Vahid Jebraeeli, Hamid Krim, Keisuke Fujii*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We seek a hierarchical resolution of residual error under a composite learning objective as a principled mechanism for identifying and exploiting additional information, particularly under distribution shift and perturbation. Inspired by the System 1/System 2 paradigm, we formulate this principle as a two-loop mechanism serving a common inference goal. A fast, System 1-like loop resolves the residual addressable by the available evidence; the remaining epistemic residual triggers a medium speed, System 2-like loop that actively acquires additional evidence and measures its informational value through residual reduction. Over nested solution spaces, the medium-loop optimum is provably no worse than the fast-loop optimum. Experiments show that the resulting two-loop model consistently improves upon the fast loop at approximately half the computation of the medium loop, including under unseen corruptions, and, when provided a cleaner observation, outperforms staged classifiers and learning-to-defer methods at equal computational cost.


### [59] [RA$^2$: Retain-Anchored Attraction Preserves Forget-Adjacent Utility in LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19385)
*Zinan Ling, Yang Xiao, Ruimeng Ye, Sijia Liu, Bo Hui*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Large Language Model (LLM) unlearning is often evaluated primarily by target suppression and clean retain utility. These metrics can miss a local failure mode: a model may perform well on standard retain prompts, yet degrade on benign reasoning when prompts lie near the forgotten domain. This phenomenon is termed forget-adjacent utility loss. A fundamental question is: where should forget representations go after they leave the removed behavior? Successful unlearning depends not only on whether forget-conditioned representations depart from the harmful computation, but also on the direction in which they are redirected afterward. We propose RA$^2$ (Retain-Anchored Attraction), a latent-space unlearning method that redirects forget-token states toward nearby retain-supported representations while preserving higher-layer behavior on retain data. On WMDP and MUSE, RA$^2$ yields higher forget-adjacent utility than strong unlearning baselines at similar broad-utility levels. These results show that the migration direction of forget representations materially affects benign behavior near the forget boundary. Our code is available at this https URL .


### [60] [Fully sequential Gaussian process regression for preference learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19391)
*Ilya O. Ryzhov*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We study continuous global optimization problems in which the objective function cannot be directly evaluated; rather, we observe only which of two selected design points appears to perform better, subject to noise. Such comparisons complicate the use of Gaussian process regression because they yield non-Gaussian posteriors. We consider a fully sequential approximation, based on moment-matching, that yields a fast closed-form update of the metamodel's mean and covariance. We then propose DOVE, a fully sequential sampling policy that combines an information gain criterion with randomized sampling designed to control error accumulation. Under fairly standard regularity conditions, we prove convergence rates for both statistical error and expected simple regret that depend on kernel smoothness. To our knowledge, these are the first such rates for fully sequential approximate Bayesian inference in continuous global optimization.


### [61] [Selective Next-Location Prediction with MetaModelSelect: A Lightweight Post-Hoc Confidence Metamodel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19394)
*Calvin Jary, Nafiseh Kahani*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Next-location prediction can support proactive mobile-network decisions, but acting on unreliable predictions can waste resources. We apply MetaModelSelect, a lightweight post-hoc confidence metamodel, to selective cellular next-location prediction. The mobility-specific model combines a learned embedding of the predicted location with the four largest softmax probabilities and Softmax Concentration. It learns whether a frozen recurrent neural network's top-1 prediction is correct, using only its outputs and without retraining the backbone. On path-balanced Changchun cellular mobility trajectories, we evaluate backbones trained with cross-entropy (CE) and self-adaptive training plus entropy minimization (SAT+EM), comparing against eight scalar confidence scores, two location-aware lookup methods, and a raw-logit multilayer perceptron. Relative to maximum softmax probability, MetaModelSelect achieves mean per-run reductions in area under the risk--coverage curve (AURC), excess AURC (E-AURC), and area under the generalized risk--coverage curve (AUGRC) of 2.6%, 4.2%, and 3.0% for CE, and 2.0%, 3.3%, and 1.9% for SAT+EM. It also achieves the lowest reported mean risk at every evaluated partial coverage. The results show that MetaModelSelect improves confidence ranking in this mobility setting while leaving the underlying location predictor unchanged.


### [62] [Train to Stitch: Topology-Preserving Regularization for Interoperable Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19401)
*Akshay Malhotra, Nicolas Zilberstein, Shahab Hamidi-Rad*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: As large, powerful pre-trained models become more prevalent, there is growing interest in developing methods that allow different neural networks to be seamlessly combined with little or no direct communication between them. Take autoencoder-based data compression as an example: the ability to independently choose architectures and train encoder and decoder models, while still ensuring they work together, could fundamentally transform how such models are built, deployed, and updated. In this work, we introduce a novel approach that leverages topological regularization to align the latent spaces of two independently trained autoencoder models, entirely eliminating the need for coordination during training. We provide a conditional theoretical analysis showing how the structural agreement encouraged by our regularizer can translate into approximate geometric compatibility between independently learned representations. Reconstruction experiments validate this approach empirically, substantially narrowing the interoperability gap across multiple datasets.


### [63] [W-JEPA: Wavelet Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture for Wearable Activity Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19411)
*Yogesh Prabhu, Loki Natarajan, Jingjing Zou*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: A major challenge in wearable device activity recognition is the heterogeneity across datasets in sensor placement, channel count, sampling rate and subjects. Moreover, the motion patterns that distinguish activities often appear as subtle, scale-specific variations within otherwise similar waveforms. As a result, raw signals are often ambiguous, and models trained on them tend to rely on cues specific to a particular sensor setup or participant, which limits both cross-dataset transfer and generalization to unseen participants. Addressing this requires representations that preserve such activity-relevant, scale-specific variation. We introduce W-JEPA, a self-supervised framework that exploits the multiscale structure of inertial motion and leverages a joint-embedding predictive architecture, by organizing windows into a fixed grid of wavelet tokens indexed by scale, sensor axis, and time. These wavelet tokens help expose activity-specific detail within broadly similar raw signals, and serve as the units of masked latent prediction. W-JEPA predicts masked token representations supplied by an exponential-moving-average (EMA) target encoder. Because recovering a masked token requires relating motion at one scale and axis to motion at other scales and axes, the model learns the cross-scale, cross-axis structure of human movement. Across six wearable activity benchmarks, W-JEPA outperforms state-of-the-art methods under random-window splits and remains the strongest when subjects are held out, improving average macro-F1 by 8.7 percentage points over the strongest published baseline. These results indicate that physically grounded multiscale tokenization, combined together with latent predictive learning, provides a promising foundation for transferable representations of heterogeneous wearable inertial data.


### [64] [Comprehensive Comparisons of Reinforcement Learning Algorithms in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19429)
*Dechen Xie, Mushuang Liu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: This paper presents a comprehensive comparison of four representative reinforcement learning (RL) methods in autonomous vehicle (AV) decision-making, including: Fitted Value Iteration (FVI), Rollout Classification Policy Iteration (RCPI), Deep Q-Network (DQN), and a discrete-action adaptation of Deep Deterministic Policy Gradient (D-DDPG). The four methods are applied to the same AV decision-making problem at an unsignalized intersection and are compared in terms of optimality and safety, training stability, and training and decision cost. RCPI is the closest to a reference solution and has the most stable training process. However, it is the least data-efficient. D-DDPG reaches the second-smallest gap to a reference solution with the highest data efficiency, but has the least training stability. DQN is the most stable model-free method but has the highest collision rate. FVI achieves a lower collision rate than DQN, but is less data-efficient and less stable. Based on these results, we provide suggestions for selecting a method according to whether a model is available, the budget for training, and whether stable training is required.


### [65] [Progress-Aware Distillation for Mitigating Stagnation in Small Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19439)
*Yuanpu Cao, Saket Sathe, Hanyu Wang, Ziyi Yin, Fenglong Ma, Jinghui Chen*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: While LLM-based agent systems have demonstrated remarkable proficiency in reasoning and solving complex real-world tasks, they also come with substantial token consumption and inference latency, limiting their practical deployment. To enhance the efficiency of agent systems, recent efforts have focused on transferring agentic capabilities from large-scale models to small language models (SLMs) via supervised fine-tuning-based distillation. Nevertheless, as interaction trajectories grow longer, involving extended reasoning-action chains and extensive environmental feedback, distilled student agents tend to experience progress stagnation: becoming trapped in unproductive loops characterized by persistent failed strategies and difficulty making meaningful progress. Our systematic analysis shows that this phenomenon consistently occurs across diverse SLMs and task domains. To address this issue, we propose Progress-Aware Distillation (ProD), which explicitly detects and penalizes stagnation in student-generated trajectories. ProD encodes the stagnation gap between student and teacher into an adaptive margin for dynamically penalizing possible stagnation. By iteratively applying this procedure, ProD enables student agents to progressively approximate teacher behavior. Extensive experiments involving 8 SLMs, ranging from 0.6B to 8B parameters, on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks, demonstrate that ProD substantially mitigates progress stagnation while enhancing both the success rates and task-completion efficiency of distilled SLM agents.


### [66] [MIPS: Mitigating Mode Collapse in Reinforcement Learning for Large Outcome Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19457)
*Armaan Sandhu, Abhijeet Sinha, Dianbo Liu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Expected-reward maximization in reinforcement learning concentrates probability mass on a few outcomes, even in tasks that require many distinct, high-quality solutions, a failure known as mode collapse. Inverse Probability Scaling (IPS) counters this in group-based policy gradients such as GRPO by dividing each reward by the outcome's probability, estimated from its frequency within the sampled group. When the outcome space is large relative to the group, outcomes rarely repeat and these estimates become uninformative. Since an outcome can be constructed along many trajectories, its probability is a sum over all of them, and computing it directly is intractable. We propose Marginal IPS (MIPS), which performs marginal evaluation by combining the sampled trajectory with a learned model of the trajectories that lead to each outcome. On Hypergrids, where the exact sampling distribution is computable, MIPS stays near the reward-distribution target as the outcome space grows relative to the rollout group, while frequency-based IPS degrades. In molecular synthesis and phylogenetic inference, MIPS sustains diverse, high-quality sampling where GRPO and frequency-based IPS collapse, showing that MIPS serves as a reliable solution to mode collapse in RL.


### [67] [SKG: A Law-Induced Engine from Inference to Induction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19505)
*Jian An, Chaoshi Wu, Jinhui Wu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: This paper proposes the SKG (Symplectic-Kernel Gradient) reasoning engine for law-induced inference and induction. Laws are a priori; data provide instances. Inference is geometric evolution under law constraints. We organize symplectic geometry, GENERIC, kernel methods, RKHS, group-equivariant networks, and constrained mechanics into a unified, analyzable, implementable, verifiable framework. We propose the SKG octuple S = (M, g, omega, H, S, G, Delta, Pi), whose four components correspond to four levels of laws: conservation laws, constitutive relations, symmetry groups, and axiom systems. Main results: (1) Invariants enter metric construction via the Jacobian: g = J^T Sigma^-1 J + epsilon_G I. For constant cases, we establish inverse reconstruction theory: solvability rank condition (Thm 3.9), consistency criterion (Thm 3.11), and uniqueness theorem (Thm 3.14). (2) The kernel bandwidth matrix is uniquely determined by Fisher information and satisfies task-independence (Thm 3.19). (3) The metric reconstruction error decomposes into four terms (Thm 3.21), with squared condition number kappa(Sigma^*)^2 as the dominant amplification factor (Thm 3.22). (4) For state-dependent cases, we establish function-space reconstruction theory on matrix-valued RKHS (Thms 4.8, 4.9). (5) Three-layer projections handle axiom, symplectic-step, and conservation constraints. (6) The residual equation yields the observable formula, four-dimensional rank condition, and noise propagation, while g_qq is structurally unidentifiable. (7) Kernel parameterization yields the RKHS extrapolation bound (Thm 4.15) and strict OOD gain theorem in group directions (Thm 8.15). (8) The learning-decoupling theorem (Thm 9.1) proves the sole object of induction is the parameters of the task energy. Validation: 1D damped oscillator and 2D rotation-invariant system; 6 unit tests, 10 experiments (paired t-test, p < 0.01).


### [68] [CktHGVAE: A Symmetric Design/State Heterogeneous Graph Variational Autoencoder for Transistor-level Analog/Mixed-signal Circuit Design and Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19508)
*Shigeyasu Uno*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We propose CktHGVAE, a heterogeneous graph variational autoencoder for transistor-level analog/mixedsignal CMOS circuits that takes not only design variables but also state variables as its inputs and outputs. With a single hyperparameter configuration without topology-specific tuning, comparison between model-generated and SPICE-simulated circuit operational states gives median R2 values above 0.9 for typical single-ended voltage amplifiers and the CMOS inverter, and 0.81-0.96 for differential amplifiers with larger datasets. Latent-space optimization in the PCA-reduced latent space with Bayesian optimization, evolutionary search, random search, and quasi-exhaustive search is demonstrated for a common-source voltage amplifier with NMOS input and resistive load, where the objective function is defined as a penalty for deviation from required circuit specifications. For each search strategy, 93.3 - 100% of all design candidates suggested by the model satisfy the required circuit specifications at the decoder output, and SPICE simulations verify that 90 - 100% of them indeed satisfy the requirements. The model pretrained on a batch of topologies can be transferred to unseen topologies without retraining at all, with zero-shot reconstruction median R2 values of 0.72-0.90 in three of four scenarios, including transfer from single-ended to differential topologies, although the transfer fidelity depends on the choice of pretraining topologies. Our demonstrations suggest that the proposed model can potentially serve as a basis for AMS CMOS circuit design automation.


### [69] [HankelKANet: Lightweight Path Loss Estimation via Physics-Inspired Hankel Convolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19524)
*Yue Ruan, Xiaoxiao Cheng, Yunan Zhu, Wenlong Huang, Meng Hua*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Accurate path loss (PL) estimation is essential for wireless network planning and optimization. This paper proposes HankelKANet, a lightweight physics-inspired network for PL estimation that structurally embeds electromagnetic propagation characteristics into the neural architecture. Built on a single residual UNet backbone, the proposed model integrates urban layout features with a convolutional Kolmogorov-Arnold network(KAN). Specifically, the convolutional kernels are parameterized by the radial amplitudes of the zeroth- and first-order Hankel functions. This formulation acts as a physics-inspired nonlinear distance encoding, enabling the network to capture dominant attenuation characteristics directly without requiring additional physics-based loss terms. Experimental results demonstrate that HankelKANet achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and efficiency on both the simulated RadioMapSeer dataset and the real-world Huawei RSRPSet. Compared with the leading baseline, PEFNet, it reduces the parameter count by 78% and lowers the RMSE by 16.96% on the simulated data, while further exhibiting robust cross-dataset transferability and high data efficiency during target-domain adaptation. To interpret these empirical gains, we mathematically establish a parameter-conditional spatial gradient bound. Using discrete Dirichlet energy (DDE) and Jacobian sensitivity heatmaps, we empirically verify that this physical encoding intrinsically constrains the network's spatial derivatives. This structural smoothness effectively eliminates non-physical spatial ripples, preventing overfitting to simulator artifacts and serving as the fundamental driver for robust real world generalization.


### [70] [Diagnosing and Correcting Reward Hacking in Verifiable Instruction Following](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19525)
*Sangyeon Yoon, Wooseok Seo, Hamish Ivison, Hyesoo Hong, Donghoon Kim, Wonjun Kang, Albert No, Jaehyung Kim*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Verifiable instruction-following benchmarks emphasize output-constraint satisfaction but can overlook whether responses meaningfully answer the underlying request, creating an evaluation blind spot. We show that frontier models can receive full credit for responses with little substantive answer content. Using the same verifier-based criterion as a training reward can induce reward hacking, as publicly released checkpoints exhibit higher constraint satisfaction but lower response quality after reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards. To address this reward hacking, we introduce CALIF, a lightweight method that augments verifier rewards with a calibrated likelihood-based bonus to preserve response quality. CALIF evaluates response likelihood under the request alone using the existing frozen reference policy, requiring no additional judge or learned reward model. Across models ranging from 1.7B to 8B in reasoning and non-reasoning modes, CALIF improves constraint satisfaction on IFBench while maintaining response quality near or above pre-RL levels. It also outperforms corresponding base policies in 40 of 45 model-metric comparisons across three rubric-based instruction-following benchmarks, demonstrating gains beyond training verifiers.


### [71] [Two-stage Residual Learning for Modelling Strength-Informed Optimisation of Thermal Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19539)
*Talha Ansar, Waqar Muhammad Ashraf, Abdulelah S. Alshehri, Omar Bayomie*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Aggregate performance metrics do not identify local modelling inaccuracies in machine learning (ML) surrogates used for thermal-system optimisation. This study assesses local modelling strength through validation-based reliability screening and post-optimisation residual assessment. A two-stage residual learning (TSRL) framework, built on artificial neural networks (ANNs), is developed for modelling-strength-informed and domain-constrained optimisation of thermal systems. Within the TSRL framework, a base ANN predicts the process output, and a residual ANN learns the signed residuals left by the base ANN. The TSRL framework is implemented on two case studies of thermal systems, including 817 simulation observations of a crude distillation unit and operating records from a 395 MW industrial gas turbine. The TSRL framework reduces the test root mean square error (RMSE) from 10.3 g/s to 4.7 g/s for Fuel Oil in the refinery dataset and from 151 kJ/kWh to 105 kJ/kWh for turbine heat rate (THR). The base ANNs trained within TSRL are embedded in domain-constrained optimisation problems, and the modelling strength at each computed solution is assessed using the locked residual-score threshold. Each computed solution receives a good modelled region (GMR) or weak modelled region (WMR) label to inform ML model-based decision-making for safety-critical operation of thermal systems.


### [72] [Test-Time Forecasting: Recurrent Depth Enables In-Context Dynamics Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19552)
*Jeffrey B. Lai, John Quinn, Anthony Bao, William Gilpin*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Foundation models for dynamical systems can forecast unseen partial differential equations (PDEs), ordinary differential equations (ODEs), and time series during inference. However, it remains an open question whether these foundation models truly learn generalizable in-context forecasting strategies. We show that looped transformers are capable of emulating propagators for dynamical systems in-context by first establishing a link between linear attention and (extended) dynamic mode decomposition. We then generalize this insight into the $\textit{Test-Time Forecasting}$ (TTF) framework in which a one-step forecaster leverages iterations of gradient descent as recurrent depth. We demonstrate that lightweight TTF models trained on ODEs and PDEs generalize to out-of-distribution system dynamics during autoregressive rollout after being trained for one-step prediction. Our results reinforce TTF as a theoretically motivated framework for designing recurrent depth models capable of forecasting unseen dynamical systems at test-time.


### [73] [TEX-MEX: Similarity-Guided Temporal Contrastive Learning for Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19557)
*Aditya Radhakrishnan, Maxwell A. Xu, Wanting Mao, Santosh Kumar, Harish Haresamudram, James M. Rehg*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Contrastive learning for time series depends on deciding which pairs of inputs should be treated as similar and dissimilar. Because few augmentations reliably preserve meaning in time series, prior methods have relied on two disjoint notions of similarity between windows: structural similarity, which relates windows with similar morphology, and temporal similarity, which relates windows that occur close together in time. We introduce TEX-MEX, to our knowledge the first approach to unify the two into a single relationship. Given a random batch of windows drawn across the dataset, TEX-MEX uses the encoder to identify which windows are structurally similar or dissimilar to each other. It then pulls each window toward the temporally shifted versions of similar windows and pushes it away from those of dissimilar ones, learning what typically accompanies similar windows in time. Across PPG, EEG, ECG, accelerometry, and continuous glucose monitoring, TEX-MEX broadly achieves state-of-the-art performance against contrastive, reconstructive, JEPA-based, and modality-specific approaches, establishing it as a strong general SSL objective. We further train a 560K-parameter PPG foundation model that achieves state-of-the-art performance across a broad range of downstream benchmarks while being roughly 50x smaller than the next-best unimodally pretrained PPG foundation model in our evaluation. We will release this foundation model open-source and open-weight to the community to support future work and practical applications.


### [74] [Learning Across Temporal Graph Representation Silos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19576)
*Minwoo Yu, Young-guk Ha*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Temporal graph data are recorded in different representations, but whether these differences also determine the scope of learned knowledge reuse remains unclear. This study investigates whether predictive structure learned from relational histories can be reused between continuous-time dynamic graphs (CTDGs) and temporal knowledge graphs (TKGs). Bidirectional reuse emerges while retaining distinct input and prediction interfaces and independent vocabularies, and sources with the same representation are not always more useful. Controlled reorganizations of source histories show that the organization contributing to reuse varies across source-target conditions. We show that learned aspects of Relational Dynamics, the predictive structure present in relational histories, can be reused across conventional representation boundaries. Independently pretrained TKG and CTDG learners can also be fused at the score level to improve prediction on a third dataset without entity or relation-semantic alignment. Two proofs of concept in recommendation and security detection illustrate that external temporal sources without direct alignment can also become useful pretraining resources.


### [75] [InstructPro2: Natural Language and Biological Grounding Guided De Novo Protein Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19592)
*Charles Novak, Zhenqiao Song, Chuan Li, Jianwen Xie, Lei Li*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: The ability to generate proteins with desired functions is a central goal of computational biology. Although natural language can express diverse design objectives, existing protein generation methods often support only narrow tasks or make limited use of biological constraints. We develop InstructPro2 to translate natural-language design objectives into protein sequences while incorporating task-specific biological context. A text encoder captures the design instructions, while dedicated ligand and antigen encoders provide biological context to the sequence-generating decoder. For pretraining, we assemble InstructPro2Bench, comprising 205.7 million <function description, protein> pairs from UniProtKB. We subsequently adapt the model to ligand-binding protein and antibody design. On functional protein generation benchmarks, InstructPro2 outperforms Pinal, the strongest baseline, achieving a functional recovery F1 of 74.8 versus 62.2 and lowering global RMSD from 5.33A to 3.04A. For antibody design, the model obtains a DockQ score of 0.39, while ligand-binding protein design reaches a docking success rate of 72.6%. These findings highlight how integrating language-based instructions with biological conditioning can extend protein generation to distinct design settings.


### [76] [Beyond Static Positional Encoding: Bayesian Time-aware Attention for Non-Stationary Long-term Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19603)
*Xinjie Lan, Yunbo Mei, Leheng Zhou, Hao Qi*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Transformers have demonstrated strong performance in long-term time series forecasting (LTSF), yet modeling non-stationary temporal dependencies remains challenging. In this paper, we interpret self-attention as a Bayesian posterior over attended positions, combining a content-based prior induced by scaled dot-product similarity with a temporal-evidence likelihood induced by positional encoding. This formulation provides a probabilistic interpretation of how positional encoding shapes the temporal dependencies captured by self-attention. Building on this perspective, we derive a population-level evidence lower bound (ELBO) that characterizes the role of positional encoding in forecasting weakly stationary time series. Our analysis further highlights a limitation of static positional encoding: when temporal dependence structures vary across input windows, a fixed temporal bias must accommodate heterogeneous dynamics and can therefore be suboptimal for non-stationary forecasting. Motivated by this analysis, we propose Bayesian Time-aware Attention (BTA), which employs a mixture-of-experts (MoE) router to adaptively combine temporal kernels and generate input-conditioned, head-specific positional biases. Extensive experiments on standard LTSF benchmarks demonstrate that BTA consistently improves forecasting performance across multiple Transformer backbones and achieves competitive overall performance with modest computational overhead and stable training.


### [77] [PN-Mix: Pair-Norm Guided Mixed-Precision Key Cache Quantization for Efficient Long-Output LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19605)
*Gil Jae Song, Sungjoo Yoo*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Long-output inference in large language models suffers from severe KV cache memory and bandwidth bottlenecks. While KV cache quantization alleviates this issue, low-bit quantization (e.g., 2-bit) severely degrades model accuracy. Recent mixed-precision key-cache quantization methods mitigate this degradation by allocating higher precision to important key channels, but estimate channel saliency using only key statistics. Through attention logit perturbation analysis at the RoPE channel-pair level, we show that quantization errors in channel pairs with large query--key pair-norm products contribute significantly to logit variation. Based on this insight, we propose PN-Mix, a mixed-precision key-cache quantization method for RoPE-based decoder-only LLMs, which allocates higher precision to salient channel pairs identified once during the prefill stage and reused throughout decoding. Across standard reasoning, code-generation, and AIME benchmarks, PN-Mix with 2.26-bit average precision achieves comparable or better average accuracy than prior mixed-precision methods using 2.32--2.39 bits. Furthermore, in our Pareto sweep, PN-Mix degrades gracefully down to 1.5 bits, whereas the compared baselines exhibit sharper performance drops below 2.25 bits.


### [78] [Divergent Paths: Learning an Outcome-Predictive Representation of LLM Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19614)
*Alexander Gill, Guram Gogia, Francisco Guzmán*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: In this work, we reframe agentic trajectories as paths through a shared embedding space of actions and environmental observations. We show that successful and failed rollouts of the same task separate in this space, with simple per-task discriminators predicting held-out rollout outcomes at up to 0.95 AUROC across three agent benchmarks and three policy models. To exploit this signal across tasks, we introduce Precog-RM, a transformer-based model that meta-learns a representation in which outcome prediction can be performed in a few-shot setting. We show that Precog-RM generalizes to unseen tasks and models, and gives confident predictions of trajectory success long before termination. Deployed as an early stopping controller for Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) on Tau2-Bench, Precog-RM cuts training FLOPs by up to 47.3\% while maintaining performance comparable to baseline GRPO.


### [79] [Sparse Autoencoders Are a Shared Coordinate System for Symbolic Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19636)
*Xiaocong Yang, Xuanyu Meng, Yuji Zhang, Jiawei Han, ChengXiang Zhai*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) hold broad knowledge in one set of weights, but lack interpretability and trustworthy reasoning, especially in high-stakes use cases. A neuro-symbolic model, with its interpretable architecture and System 2 reasoning capacity, is a promising alternative if built at the scale of LLMs; however, its symbolic backbone is usually constructed with human effort, so existing neuro-symbolic models are bottlenecked by the scope of the symbol component and usually domain-specific. A data-driven method could instead build a larger symbolic system by aligning and merging existing ones. We target its first, foundational step: matching units across these heterogeneous systems. Our method, SAE-IDF, encodes a symbol in its native context into a sparse code with a sparse autoencoder (SAE), re-weights each dimension by its rarity and matches across systems. On 12 tasks across 6 kinds of symbolic system, SAE-IDF outperforms the same LLM's dense baseline on all tasks with Gemma-2-9B and all but one with Llama-3.1-8B, by up to 45 points, consistently across SAE layers and configurations. SAE-IDF is also interpretable: every dimension of the sparse code has a human-readable meaning, so each match decomposes into named concepts (features). Our work paves the way to a universal symbolic backbone.


### [80] [Synthetic reaction data from transformation rules for graph-to-sequence pretraining in biodegradation pathway prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19640)
*Irsyaad Rijwan, Liam Brydon, Aidan Jared, Jörg Simon Wicker*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: The scarcity of observed biodegradation reactions constrains the training of product and pathway predictors. We present enviGraph, a framework that generates and curates synthetic reaction data, uses these data to pretrain a graph-to-sequence predictor, and fine-tunes the predictor on observed environmental reactions for product and recursive pathway prediction. Applying inherited environmental transformation rules to structures from PubChem, NORMAN SusDat and EPA DSSTox, followed by curation, produces 776,851 canonical parent-product pairs for pretraining. We compare this dataset with three United States Patent and Trademark Office (USPTO) reaction datasets: USPTO-50K, USPTO-MIT and USPTO-Stereo. We evaluate enviGraph and the sequence-based enviFormer using ten folds split by pathway on the EAWAG Biocatalysis/Biodegradation Database (BBD), regulatory pesticide biodegradation data (Soil) and activated-sludge biodegradation data (Sludge). For both predictors, synthetic pretraining gives the highest BBD mean precision-recall area under the curve (PR-AUC) among the evaluated pretraining datasets for single-generation and multi-generation prediction. enviGraph achieves BBD single-generation PR-AUC of $0.202 \pm 0.050$ with synthetic pretraining, compared with $0.169 \pm 0.065$ for the retrospectively selected strongest USPTO condition, USPTO-Stereo (mean $\pm$ sample standard deviation across folds). Its exact BBD top-1 and top-5 accuracies with synthetic pretraining are 0.355 and 0.594. The strongest USPTO conditions give higher PR-AUC means than synthetic pretraining in most Soil and Sludge comparisons. enviGraph has higher Soil multi-generation PR-AUC means than enviFormer under each of the four main pretraining datasets, and with synthetic pretraining it also leads enviFormer on BBD single-generation PR-AUC and exact top-1 and top-5 accuracy.


### [81] [On Global Joint Lower Bounds in Zeroth-Order Nonconvex Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19652)
*Yuxuan Xia, Xiao-Tong Yuan*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Recent work has sought to transfer first-order nonconvex optimization guarantees to zeroth-order methods through gradient estimators, typically incurring a factor linear in $d$. \citet{kornowski2024} obtained an $\mathcal{O}(d\delta^{-1}\epsilon^{-3})$ oracle complexity for nonsmooth nonconvex optimization and asked whether its dependence on all three parameters is jointly optimal. Although the dimension dependence and the first-order dependence on $(\delta,\epsilon)$ are separately known to be unavoidable, a matching zeroth-order lower bound has remained open. We answer this question in the unrestricted full-space setting. For smooth stochastic nonconvex optimization, we establish a scalar-oracle lower bound of $\Omega\!\left(dL_0^2\min\{\Delta H,L_0^2\}/\epsilon^4\right)$ assuming a smooth, globally lower-bounded population objective and a second-moment bound on sample Lipschitz constants. A smooth reduction then yields a lower bound for Goldstein stationarity, proving the joint minimax optimality of the dependence on $(d,\delta,\epsilon)$ under explicit high-dimensional and small-accuracy conditions. Crucially, our results allow algorithms to query arbitrary points in $\mathbb{R}^d$, adaptively select sample-function labels, reuse the same sampled function at any number of locations, and output points without spatial restrictions. Thus, unlike lower bounds based on bounded query regions or fresh evaluation noise, our guarantees apply to a fully adaptive, reusable-sample scalar oracle. We also establish a separate noiseless exact-value lower bound against randomized algorithms, and hence also against deterministic methods. Our proof combines a globally defined sequential nonconvex construction with an exact Gaussian scalar channel, multistage random-field estimates, and an antithetic finite-sample realization that preserves information hardness under adaptive reuse.


### [82] [Enhancing Latent World Models with Associative Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19683)
*Yuwei Sun, Siyu Zhu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: World models trained via latent dynamics offer a scalable path for embodied planning, yet existing methods suffer from representational collapse, computational overhead, and limited long-horizon capability. We introduce a memory-enhanced latent world model that augments JEPA-style prediction with a content-addressable memory bank. Our approach learns high-level representations directly from observations, operating at multiple temporal scales within a shared latent space, and conditions predictions via adaptive layer normalization. To prevent collapse and ensure effective memory usage, we design two complementary losses that encourage slot diversity and balanced temporal utilization. Experiments on PushT, Tworoom, Reacher, and OGBCube demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance with a lightweight architecture, outperforming conventional latent world models on long-horizon planning tasks.


### [83] [Beyond Distribution Matching: Selective Margin Preservation for Supervised Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19685)
*Yuxuan Wang, Guangxiang Zhao, Xinyu Tan, Yahui Han, Xinxiang Yin, Zhonghai Wu, Xiangzheng Zhang, Lin Sun, Tong Yang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) changes logit margins among non-target tokens at teacher-forced prefixes. Matching a local reference distribution keeps those probabilities and also penalizes margin expansions. We ask whether a soft auxiliary can preserve selected local non-target margin relations without freezing that distribution. We introduce \textbf{Selective Rank-Preserving SFT (SRP-SFT)}, a soft margin target: a hinge on reference-scaled lower bounds for pairs from the reference's top-$K$ non-target tokens, applied at fixed teacher-forced prefixes. The hinge stops once the requested bound is met. We derive the KL cost of violating a margin, characterize the feasible region and its conditional utility, and give a finite-step condition for reducing aggregate violations. In one-epoch experiments on Qwen3-4B-Base and UltraFeedback, this target raises MATH-500 and code-generation scores over CE and LP-SFT at all three training seeds. At seed 1234, MATH-500 pass@1 rises from 49.56\% to 54.56\% and the four-metric code mean rises from 65.91\% to 69.50\% relative to LP-SFT. Ranking controls, held-out cache diagnostics, sensitivity sweeps, and six additional backbones show where these score changes appear.


### [84] [Function-Space Temporal Self-Distillation for Policy Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19687)
*Aryan Das, Arnab Bhattacharya, Vinay Kumar Verma*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) improves reasoning in language models, but scalar outcomes provide coarse supervision over response trajectories. Self-distillation offers denser token-level guidance by comparing an unassisted policy with a feedback-informed self-teacher, yet the teacher evolves with the student, creating unstable targets and motivating additional regularization. We introduce Function-Space Policy Optimization (FSPO), a single-policy framework that combines current privileged adaptation with function-space temporal consolidation. FSPO stores sparse historical next-token distributions as fixed temporal targets, providing temporal separation without maintaining a persistent teacher model. It further introduces privileged disagreement correction to explicitly suppress locally overvalued actions, reliability-adaptive grounding that strengthens reference supervision when self-generated evidence repeatedly fails, and quality and coverage-aware temporal memory that retains verified behaviors and improves privileged demonstrations. Across reasoning and tool-use benchmarks, FSPO achieves the highest average accuracy under a 5-hour training budget, improving over the strongest baseline by approximately 4% points on average while reducing peak memory from about 70 GB to 56 GB on Olmo3-7B-Instruct.


### [85] [Annealed Lévy Diffusion via Gaussian Surrogate Reverse Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19692)
*Yuling Jiao, Lican Kang, Jerry Zhijian Yang, Cheng Yuan, Ruizhe Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Gaussian diffusion models offer a practical framework for generative modeling. Denoising Lévy Probabilistic Models (DLPM) introduce heavy-tailed perturbations with a fixed tail index throughout denoising. We introduce Annealed Lévy Diffusion (ALD). ALD varies the stability index $\alpha(t)$ to combine heavy-tailed exploration with Gaussian refinement. Our marginal-first formulation represents stable noise as a scalar Gaussian scale mixture. This formulation enables standard Gaussian noise-prediction training with one additional scale input. We study two sampling processes. ALD-renoise estimates the clean sample and injects fresh marginal noise. ALD-posterior preserves dependence between successive states through an accumulated conditional variance and a Gaussian bridge update. Both samplers reuse the same trained predictor with different scalar inputs. We evaluate ALD on four 2D benchmarks and an imbalanced nine-mode mixture. We also examine training efficiency and the effects of prediction perturbations on clipped Holtsmark data. Additional experiments evaluate MNIST and CelebA64 generation and CIFAR-10LT augmentation. ALD preserves local geometry on the evaluated light-tailed and manifold-structured targets.


### [86] [ReGRA: Residual-Gradient Adaptation for Complementary Low-Rank Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19700)
*Fanyao Li, Yuxi Liu, Xiaohao Liu, He Chang, Zhaoxin Wang, Zhulin Tao*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) enables parameter-efficient fine-tuning by learning low-rank weight updates. Increasing rank expands adaptation capacity, but does not guarantee that all learned directions improve downstream performance. In a diagnostic experiment with rank-16 LoRA, we show that retaining only the top singular components achieves the best performance, whereas adding further components degrades it. Motivated by this, we propose Residual-Gradient Adaptation (ReGRA), a two-stage method for making more effective use of a fixed rank budget. After the first stage, ReGRA estimates task gradients on a calibration set and removes their projections onto the two-sided subspace induced by the first-stage update. It then aggregates the residual-gradient directions to initialize a second-stage adapter without changing the first-stage update. An orthogonality regularizer encourages the two updates to capture complementary directions. Both updates can be merged into the pretrained weights, incurring no additional inference overhead. Across diverse benchmark sets, ReGRA achieves stronger performance than alternatives with the same total rank and even matches LoRA adapters with twice the rank. Ablation studies and further analysis support the contribution of its core design choices. Code is available at this https URL .


### [87] [New Model, Old Habits: Behavior Transfer under Model Swapping in Multi-Session LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19712)
*Youngbin Choi, Sehyun Hwang, Suhyeon Jun, Seoyeon Lee, Sunghyun Choi, MoonJeong Park, Dongwoo Kim*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Multi-session agents can switch between LLMs to balance performance and inference cost. Such switches require transferring a history for continuity, yet their effects on the recipient remain underexplored. Through controlled experiments with multi-session tasks, we uncover behavior transfer: recipients tend to adopt reasoning and action patterns from the models that generated their histories. This effect is most evident with raw-history transfer but is substantially reduced by summarization. Based on these findings, we propose Direction- and Authorship-aware Summarization of History (DASH) that preserves stronger-model behavior while minimizing weak-to-strong transfer via summarization. Experiments with repeated swaps show that DASH outperforms raw-history transfer and naive summarization baselines. These results suggest that effective multi-model orchestration requires considering not only which model to use, but also which behavioral patterns to carry forward.


### [88] [TIMERSI: Towards Self-Improved Agentic Deployment for Time-Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19725)
*Haoxin Liu, Deqing Fu, Lucheng Fu, Yiqiao Jin, Rushi Qiang, Harshavardhan Kamarthi, B. Aditya Prakash*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Time-series foundation models provide strong zero-shot forecast, but deployment for real- world applications still requires task-specific designs for model inputs and outputs. Direct inference can leave domain knowledge and re- curring forecast errors unaddressed, while tai- lored pipelines require substantial human ex- pertise and effort. We introduce TIMERSI, a self-improving agentic deployment system around frozen forecasting models. A cod- ing agent translates task descriptions into ex- ecutable pipeline changes. It tests each re- vision on historical forecasting windows and uses observed errors to guide subsequent re- visions. During deployment, an adaptive se- lector chooses among the resulting pipelines using completed forecasts under similar con- ditions. Across 198 tasks on TIME and FEV- bench, TIMERSI outperforms all submissions on six point and probabilistic forecasting met- rics. Controlled comparisons support the value of feedback-guided revision over non-iterative pipeline search. Further experiments verify the generalization with different coding agents and forecasting models.


### [89] [What Happens to the Goal? Distinct Failures of Policy Alignment in Goal Misgeneralization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19731)
*Julia Barth, Armin Sommer, Kunvar Thaman, Jannik Schilling*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Goal misgeneralization is a common failure mode in which an agent behaves competently out of distribution but does not pursue the intended goal. However, we still have a weak understanding of what happens to the intended goal in misgeneralizing agents. Across three Procgen environments and five robot tasks, we find three distinct forms of policy-goal misalignment: (1) functional target substitution, where behavior follows an alternative goal, (2) a policy shortcut, where behavior follows a learned action strategy, and (3) cue competition between intended and proxy goal cues. We distinguish these regimes by tracing where goal information remains available internally, how strongly it influences the policy, and what role it plays in action selection, and find that the goal is lost at different depths. Where the goal reaches the decision, as in cue competition, steering redirects the policy to the goal's location, raising intended-goal reach from 16.0% to 95.3%. Where it reaches the policy but not the decision, only new downstream wiring recovers goal reach, from 31.6% to 84.3% in the environment with the action shortcut. Where it is inert, neither works. Our results show that goal misgeneralization is not a single failure of goal representation, but distinct failures of policy alignment at different depths, tightly connected to which recovery works.


### [90] [ProtModernBERT: Compute-Efficient Protein Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19738)
*Alberto Martínez, Natalia Flechas Manrique, Llorenç Espinosa-Portalés, Luc Andrea*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Large protein language models provide a powerful framework for learning sequence-function relationships and guiding experimental design. However, architectural improvements are underexplored, especially in low-compute regimes of relevance for resource-constrained research laboratories. We show that the training cost for encoder-only transformer architectures can be reduced by leveraging modern architectural refinements, offering an encouraging performance / compute-efficiency trade-off. We introduce ProtModernBERT, a modernized BERT-style model pre-trained on amino acid sequences, and compare it with ProtBert, Amplify, and ESM2 across a set of supervised structural tasks -encompassing secondary structure prediction, disorder prediction, and subcellular localization- and ProteinGym's zero-shot functional benchmark. We find that ProtModernBERT matches or closely tracks the performance of Amplify-120M and ProtBert with up to 80-90\% reduction in pre-training FLOPs, and narrows the gap to ESM2 when controlling for pre-training. Our analysis using a cluster-based validation protocol for hyperparameter selection and randomly initialized models disentangles the contributions of architecture and data, showing that modern components provide stronger inductive biases that are more consequential than parameter count in low-compute regimes.


### [91] [CAST: History-Adaptive Spectral Clipping for LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19761)
*Jiacheng Li, Jianchao Tan, Kefeng Zhang, Yuchen Xie, Xunliang Cai*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Soft spectral clipping (SSC) reduces gradient singular values, but its effect depends on the gradient scale and clipping threshold. We introduce CAST (Clipping with Adaptive Scales and Thresholds), a history-adaptive extension of SSC. AdamW's previous second moment sets row and column scales. An exponential moving average of the clipped output's estimated spectral norm sets each matrix's threshold. CAST restores the original coordinates before the AdamW update. We train a mixture-of-experts (MoE) model with 19.08B total parameters for 83.89B tokens. CAST achieves lower training and mean validation losses than nine gradient-processing baselines, with the fewest relative gradient spikes. It reaches the best baseline's final smoothed training loss in 7.2\% fewer steps.


### [92] [Learning to Compute Is Not Learning to Retain or Reuse in Looped Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19768)
*Peilong Zhou, Hongtao Zhang, Yulong Huang, Cangyuan Li, Mingyu Chen, Ying Wang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Looped transformers compute by applying one block repeatedly, so they can run for more loops than they were trained with. Recurrent reasoners are known to overthink: iterating past the trained depth can turn a correct answer into a wrong one. Such a late error can reflect failed computation, loss of retention, or a state that later computation cannot reuse. We separate these cases in in-context Mealy-machine execution, where a simulator supplies intermediate targets and exact counterfactuals for hidden-state interventions. Models trained to answer at loop 32 are fully correct there but keep only 22% of these answers at loop 64. Retention depends on what training asks for: supervising one randomly chosen position per example, instead of always the last, keeps 99.8%, and randomizing the number of training loops also helps; truncating gradients, as in progressive training against overthinking, does not. The computation itself is exact: position $k$ is computed at loop $k$, and swapping its loop-$k$ hidden vector for the one from another input redirects every later position to that input's exact continuation (237/237 pairs in each of ten models). Retention, however, is not reuse. Even in models that keep nearly all answers, holding each position $k$ fixed from loop $k$ drops accuracy to 4--6%, whereas holding it from loop $k+4$ is harmless. A released recurrent maze network trained to avoid overthinking behaves the same way. Learning to compute is thus not learning to retain, and avoiding overthinking does not make a state reusable when its answer first appears.


### [93] [From Global to Tail Convergence: Dynamics of $α$-Stable Score-Based Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19773)
*Ianis Hammani, Claire Boyer*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Score-based generative models driven by $\alpha$-stable L{é}vy processes have been proposed as an alternative to Brownian diffusion models, with empirical evidence suggesting improved sampling and better modeling of heavy-tailed data. Yet theoretical guarantees for these models remain limited. We develop a non-asymptotic convergence analysis for scale-preserving $\alpha$-stable generative dynamics, covering both compactly supported and power-law targets. In p-Wasserstein distance, we quantify the effects of early stopping, initialization, score approximation, and time discretization, and show exponential decay of the initialization error. A comparison with Brownian dynamics reveals that global Wasserstein convergence alone does not fundamentally distinguish the two models: both initialization errors decay exponentially with the forward horizon on the common class of heavy-tailed targets. A sharp separation emerges instead in the tails. At any positive generation time, stable dynamics produces power-law tails with index $\alpha$, whereas Brownian dynamics remains light-tailed. For a target with tail index $\nu$, asymptotically correct extreme-quantile scaling is possible precisely when $\alpha$=$\nu$. These results identify tail-index matching as a principled mechanism behind the ability of stable generative models to capture extreme events.


### [94] [Learning Who Benefits Most: Multi-Treatment Causal Ranking for Population Health Stratification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19782)
*Rocco Zaccagnino, Martina Girolamo, Alberto Lombardi, Delfina Malandrino, Donatello Telesca, Alessia Ture*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Population health stratification frameworks, such as the Italian DM77, organize patients by clinical need and care pathways but offer limited guidance on which care changes to prioritize under constrained resources. We present PROMETHEUS (Proactive Resource Optimization through Multi-treatment Effect-based Targeting in Healthcare Environments Using Stratification), a framework that formulates population health stratification as a multi-treatment causal ranking problem. Its objective is to order eligible patient-treatment opportunities by expected incremental benefit rather than obtain perfectly calibrated individual treatment-effect estimates. Each clinically admissible care intensification is compared with maintaining the patient's current pathway. Treatments are learned jointly to produce a globally comparable ranking across patients and care profiles, within domains sharing a reference condition, outcome, follow-up horizon, and benefit scale. This separates clinical need, treatment benefit, and resource-constrained allocation. PROMETHEUS combines doubly robust causal signals with a Siamese neural ranking model and contrastive representation pretraining. The shared representation captures similarities across care transitions, supporting prioritization under capacity constraints. Synthetic experiments and semi-synthetic experiments using real administrative covariates from ASL Benevento evaluate ranking quality, allocation value, robustness, and failure modes. The results support the framework's ability to recover generated benefit orderings and prioritize care opportunities in the evaluated settings. PROMETHEUS provides a preclinical approach to actionable stratification; validation with observed treatment-outcome data is required before clinical use.


### [95] [Reinforcement Learning via Advantage Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19783)
*Zhiyuan Zhou, Sergey Levine*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) holds the promise of learning a policy that can outperform what is shown in the data. However, in practice, such systems suffer from instability and hyperparameter sensitivity. In contrast, supervised learning provides a stable and scalable training objective, but is fundamentally limited by the quality of the demonstrated behavior. In this paper, we ask whether we can perform policy optimization with something as simple and stable as supervised learning but as performant as RL. Unfortunately, naively training the policy with supervised learning and conditioning on future rewards or advantages typically underperform dedicated RL methods. We introduce a new policy extraction method, Advantage Guidance, that retains the simple supervised learning structure with advantage conditioning while outperforming prior RL methods through effective conditional training and contrastive diffusion guidance during sampling. We show that Advantage Guidance can significantly improve a model's ability to respond to conditional advantage signals, and the contrastive guidance during sampling can use the learned advantage-conditioned policy to target the KL-regularized optimal policy. Empirically, we find that Advantage Guidance significantly outperforms prior state-of-the-art RL methods on 26 long-horizon, sparse reward tasks across datasets with varying degrees of optimality.


### [96] [Asymptotically Exact Negative Guidance of Diffusion Models via Positive-Unlabeled Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19802)
*Hirohane Takagi, Masashi Sugiyama*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Negative guidance of diffusion models generates samples that avoid an undesired condition, specified either by reference samples or by a conditional score alongside an unconditional model. Existing methods inject repulsive terms derived from local information at each sample, so the resulting sampler does not target an explicit density, and its bias cannot be systematically reduced by increasing computation. We instead present a negative-guidance method that samples from an explicit target density, which uniformly handles both reference-sample and score-based specifications and suppresses probability mass near the undesired condition with an avoidance strength that parametrizes the target density itself. Realizing this target requires estimating, at each diffusion timestep, how likely each sample is to belong to the undesired condition. We cast this estimation as a Positive-Unlabeled learning problem that admits lightweight online training along the diffusion trajectory, using condition samples as positives and diffusion particles as unlabeled data. Because the guided particles drift from the unconditional marginal, we further correct the residual mismatch via Sequential Monte Carlo, yielding a sampler that is asymptotically exact in the particle limit and that turns any existing negative-guidance method into its proposal kernel. Empirically, our method approaches the ground-truth target on Gaussian mixtures and Pareto-dominates existing methods on trade-offs between avoidance and generation quality, distributional bias, and diversity, including in an image-generation setting with a pretrained score network.


### [97] [Efficient contextual partial monitoring with general function approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19804)
*Kapilan Balagopalan, Hao Qin, Kwang-Sung Jun, Chicheng Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We study finite stochastic partial monitoring (PM) games with side information, a paradigm of sequential learning with partial feedback. On each round $t \in [T]$, both player and environment observe a context and player chooses an action and environment simultaneously chooses an outcome. The player neither observes the outcome nor the loss; instead, they only observe a feedback symbol which is partially informative about the outcome. We utilize the Estimation-to-Decisions (E2D) meta-algorithm of Foster et al.(2021) to design this http URL , a computationally efficient framework for contextual partial monitoring, which uses an online regression oracle to efficiently estimate the outcome distributions based on historical (context, action, feedback symbol) tuples. Since the exploration rule given by the original E2D algorithm is intractable to compute, we propose two computationally efficient exploration rules that achieve optimal regret orders in $T$: in the globally observable setting, our exploration rule is based on $\epsilon$-greedy, while in the locally observable setting, our exploration rule uses inverse gap weighting (Foster & Rakhlin, 2020), which was previously only known to be effective in the bandit setting. We show that a variant of this http URL named this http URL can be combined with offline regression oracles to give an efficient algorithm with optimal regret when the contexts are drawn independently from a fixed distribution. We evaluate our algorithm on a synthetic label-efficient prediction game and an LLM routing task, in which a learner decides from prompt, uncertainty, and internal state features whether to escalate a query from a weak model to a strong one. We show that our method has superior performance to prior approaches.


### [98] [VaryST: Generalizable Spatio-Temporal Forecasting Beyond the Closed-World Setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19813)
*Shengnan Guo, Jinjun Cheng, Xiaowei Mao, Jianhao Zhang, Haochen Lv, Yang Jiang, YiHeng Huang, Yan Lin, Youfang Lin, Huaiyu Wan*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Spatio-temporal forecasting is fundamental to many real-world systems, yet deployment environments are inherently varying: temporal distributions may evolve, spatial domains may change, and observation availability may shift from training conditions. Existing models are often tightly coupled to their training environments through node-specific representations, predefined spatial support sets, and reliance on consistently available observations, limiting generalization to changing environments. To this end, we propose VaryST, a compact spatio-temporal forecasting model trained from scratch that achieves strong generalization by dynamically constructing predictive context from available long-term observations. To accommodate temporal changes, VaryST derives node representations from long-term histories, capturing current behavior in the context of longer-term patterns. To adapt to spatial changes, it dynamically selects functionally relevant support nodes beyond predefined neighborhoods and integrates their information with recent and long-term target states and global context. To handle varying observation availability, VaryST selectively masks historical patches on which the model relies most during training, encouraging it to exploit alternative temporal and spatial dependencies for accurate forecasting when key observations become unavailable. Extensive experiments on six real-world datasets against seventeen baselines show that VaryST achieves strong in-domain forecasting accuracy, substantially improves zero- and few-shot generalization under spatial and temporal changes, and remains robust to varying observation availability.


### [99] [When Does Depth Help? Understanding Layerwise Inference in Tabular Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19814)
*Wenwen Gong, Haotian Gu, Zhen Yang, Jiaying Ma, Hanqing Zhang, Zhongshen Wang, Jingjun Wang, Shouyi Xu, Wei Wang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Transformer-based tabular foundation models (TFMs) achieve state-of-the-art performance on small to medium-sized tabular prediction tasks while processing diverse inputs through a shared stack of layers. Recent layerwise analyses identify strong predictive performance in early TFM representations and substantial overlap in computation across layers. However, early predictive accessibility alone does not establish whether later layers are dispensable for every input, and aggregate accuracy can obscure which inputs benefit from additional computation.
