Guided by these theoretical results, we introduce two asymptotically correct algorithms for Additive Noise Models (ANMs) that recover the underlying causal graph and provide consistent parameter estimates.


### [437] [Bayesian Causal Forests for Zero-Inflated Continuous Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18889)
*Hugo Gobato Souto, Eric Wang, Igor Shuryak*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Estimating (heterogeneous) treatment effects is still challenging when the outcome is semicontinuous (i.e., exhibiting both a discrete point mass at zero and a skewed continuous distribution for positive values), despite the strong presence of this data type in real world settings. Existing methods either ignore the zero mass, impose fragile parametric assumptions, or suffer from extreme computational costs and data augmentation noise. This paper proposes Zero-Inflated Continuous Bayesian Causal Forests with Duan's Smearing (ZIC-BCF-Smear), a decoupled two-part nonparametric estimator that fits a Probit BCF on the full sample to model participation and a Gaussian BCF strictly on the active subpopulation to model positive intensity. Subpopulation Propensity Adjustment (SPA) mitigates regularization-induced confounding within the active subpopulation, and Duan's non-parametric smearing estimator re-transforms predictions to the response scale, bypassing the fragile scale multipliers of parametric models. A comprehensive simulation benchmark shows that ZIC-BCF-Smear matches well-specified parametric models without their assumptions and remains stable and calibrated where competitors either diverge or pathologically over-cover for nonlinear data. The method is then applied to individual-participant data from the SPECTRUM randomized phase III head-and-neck cancer trial, estimating the causal effect of assignment to panitumumab plus chemotherapy on the number of days patients spend with severe toxicity, an outcome for which a quarter of patients contribute an exact zero. Panitumumab assignment raises that burden by about ten days per patient under the trial's own differential treatment duration, and the decoupled structure localizes the effect mainly in the positive-intensity part rather than in whether the reconstructed severe-AE burden is positive.


### [438] [Delay Complexity Dimension: Learning with Delayed and Censored Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18935)
*Madhava Gaikwad*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study PAC learning of binary classifiers when labels arrive after random delays and are censored beyond an observation window $W$. How many samples does this observation model require? We propose a distribution-dependent answer, the Delay Complexity Dimension (DCD). The DCD combines the VC dimension with the exact label-information rate of the observation channel. Every learner needs $\Omega(\mathrm{DCD}/\varepsilon^2)$ samples. An oracle pseudo-label ERM matches this rate on the instance family of the lower bound, up to constants and logarithmic factors. On that family the DCD is within a constant factor of the classical censored-ERM complexity, and whether the DCD rate is achievable where the two separate remains open. The theory also yields a quantization scheme under which delay information lowers the residual label variance, an expedition policy with a $(1-1/e)$ guarantee for its version-space objective, and a phenomenon with no analogue in the no-delay setting. Under a fixed collection horizon, a moderate window can strictly beat immediate labels. Experiments on synthetic data and the Criteo conversion logs support these predictions. A budget-matched test of a companion labeler-diversity prediction is reported as a negative result.


### [439] [Wong--Zakai Convergence and FNO Learning for Stochastic Volterra Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18939)
*Wonjae Lee, Jiuk Jang, Hyungbin Park*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: This paper studies Wong--Zakai convergence and Fourier neural operator learning for stochastic Volterra integral equations (SVIEs) describing random systems with memory. A Wong--Zakai theorem establishes convergence of pathwise solutions driven by piecewise linear approximations of Brownian motion to the corresponding SVIE solution. This convergence provides a foundation for using mirror-padded Fourier neural operators to learn the solution operator mapping initial conditions and driving noise to entire solution trajectories. Numerical experiments demonstrate competitive prediction accuracy and generalization to temporal grids finer than those used for training, while assessing the influence of padding and computational cost. As a follow-up to \citet{LKP:26}, this study extends their operator-learning framework to SVIEs through separate convergence and approximation analyses tailored to convolutional memory.


### [440] [Semi-Amortized Bayesian Inference with Reusable Local Posteriors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18945)
*Niek Stevenson, Paul-Christian Bürkner, Andrew Heathcote, Stefan T. Radev*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Amortized Bayesian inference uses simulations to perform Bayesian estimation when likelihoods are expensive or analytically unavailable. However, learning the full joint posterior is challenging in high-dimensional models with complex dependencies. We introduce a semi-amortized method that divides parameters into smaller blocks, and uses conditional independence to identify the information needed to infer each block. We train neural estimators for blocks whose posterior updates are computationally expensive or frequently repeated. Each estimator learns from simulations of the relevant model components to approximate the block's full conditional posterior, given the relevant observations and the values of the variables on which it depends. A Markov chain then alternates direct draws from these estimators with conventional updates of the remaining variables. Each neural update uses the current conditioning values and requires neither likelihood evaluation nor an acceptance-rejection step. Where the same conditional structure recurs, one estimator can be reused across blocks and datasets, with an output dimension determined by the block rather than the full model. We illustrate the approach in high-dimensional and computationally expensive hierarchical and spatiotemporal models.


### [441] [Discrete Diffusion Models are Minimax Optimal Under Sparsity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19020)
*Luca Blunda, Tyler Farghly, Umut Simsekli, Alain Durmus*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Discrete diffusion models have emerged as a leading alternative to autoregressive models for generating text and other categorical data, yet their theoretical foundations remain less developed than those of their continuous counterparts. For continuous diffusion models, a prominent line of theory suggests that these models can adapt to low-dimensional structure in the data, thereby improving statistical efficiency and generalization. In this work, we investigate the discrete analogue of this phenomenon by studying the statistical rates of discrete diffusion models under sparsity assumptions. By analyzing a nonparametric estimator derived from a regularized score-entropy objective, we establish minimax-optimal rates in the sparse regime. The proposed regularization can be viewed as an adaptation of label smoothing techniques introduced for classification, and does not require the knowledge of the sparsity parameter. This shows that discrete diffusion models can adapt intrinsically to sparsity.


### [442] [Conformalized Physics-Informed Weak Regression for Parameter Estimation in Epidemic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19058)
*Allan Peter Engsig-Karup*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Compartment models of infectious diseases are often linear in coefficients that are physical parameters or products or ratios of them. We exploit this with closed-form least squares and equip the estimates with distribution-free uncertainty. Physics-informed regression (PIR) fits the parameters to finite-difference derivatives of the data; we introduce physics-informed weak regression (PIWR), which integrates the model in time instead, needs no derivatives of the data, estimates the initial state jointly and is high-order accurate. To quantify uncertainty we wrap both estimators in split conformal prediction, with a calibration population generated by the physics-based model itself, giving simultaneous intervals for all parameters with a finite-sample coverage guarantee. A residual-adaptive factor, computed without ground truth, scales the intervals to each data set (PIWR-RACC). On the eleven benchmark models of disease-informed neural networks (DINNs), PIWR or PIR-FD6 is one to four orders of magnitude more accurate than the reported DINN results wherever the sampling resolves the dynamics, in about a millisecond per fit instead of minutes to hours of training, and the 90% intervals achieve 87-92% coverage in all 88 settings. On the S3I3R model of the original PIR study, PIWR lowers the reported PIR errors by about three orders of magnitude. On real data from the 1978 boarding-school influenza outbreak, a variable-projection variant gives calibrated 95% intervals that overlap those of a Bayesian analysis. On a time-varying transmission benchmark, a B-spline expansion of beta(t) keeps PIR and PIWR linear; they are more accurate than physics-informed neural networks in three of four cases and comparable in the noisiest, with simultaneous bands for beta(t). The complete conformal calibration takes less than two minutes for every benchmark.


### [443] [Neural Supervised Clustering for Learning Individualized Treatment Rules with Many Treatments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19104)
*Daixi Huang, Yixin Yuan, Yifan Dai, Yufeng Liu*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Learning an individualized treatment rule (ITR) with many treatments requires estimating treatment-specific effects from limited observations per arm. Treatments with similar conditional responses can share information, but linear contrast models may miss nonlinear heterogeneity. We propose nonlinear supervised clustering fusion (NSCF), a neural Q-learning framework with a shared nonlinear covariate representation and low-dimensional treatment embeddings. Pairwise fusion groups treatments with similar learned contrasts, yielding an ITR and an interpretable partition. An alternating algorithm combines neural training, embedding fusion, validation-based partition selection, and unpenalized grouped refitting. NSCF lowers regret relative to a strong neural reference in nonlinear exact-sharing simulations; perturbations characterize the limits of hard sharing. In a melanoma xenograft application, NSCF improves average policy value over the neural reference. These results support nonlinear representation learning with supervised treatment fusion when treatment effects exhibit shared structure.


### [444] [Student Copula Markov Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19135)
*Salha Mamane, Cheikh Tidiane Seck*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: This paper extends the applicability of probabilistic graphical models to non Gaussian data. It establishes a characterization of the pairwise Markov property in undirected probabilistic graphical models in terms of copula densities. Building on this characterization, we derive a necessary condition for the pairwise Markov property in Student copula Markov networks. An Expectation-Conditional Maximization algorithm (ECM) is provided to facilitate the penalized maximum likelihood estimation and graph selection. The performance of the proposed algorithm is evaluated through simulation studies, which demonstrate its effectiveness in estimating model parameters and recovering the underlying graph structure.


### [445] [Structure-aware Bayesian full waveform inversion via diffusion-regularized Stein variational gradient descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19187)
*Chao Li, Mrinal K. Sen, Sergey Fomel, Yangkang Chen*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Full waveform inversion (FWI) is a powerful tool for high-resolution subsurface model reconstruction, but it remains highly nonlinear and ill-posed, especially when the initial model is inaccurate or the acquired data are noisy and incomplete. Annealed Stein variational gradient descent (ASVGD) provides a particle-based Bayesian framework for FWI by approximating the posterior distribution with an ensemble of velocity models, thereby enabling uncertainty quantification beyond a single deterministic solution. However, conventional ASVGD-FWI commonly relies on simple handcrafted priors, such as Gaussian or smoothing assumptions, which are often insufficient to represent complex geological structures. To address this limitation, we introduce a learned diffusion model (DM) as an implicit regularization mechanism within the ASVGD-FWI framework. Instead of explicitly evaluating the diffusion prior density, the trained diffusion model acts as a learned geological projection operator that guides updated particles toward the distribution of plausible velocity models. In this way, the proposed method combines data consistency from FWI, posterior diversity from ASVGD, and structural realism from the diffusion prior. Numerical experiments demonstrate that the diffusion-regularized ASVGD-FWI produces more geologically consistent mean models, suppresses nonphysical artifacts, and yields uncertainty estimates that are more concentrated around structurally complex and poorly constrained regions. These results suggest that learned diffusion priors can effectively enhance Bayesian FWI by improving both inversion stability and the interpretability of posterior uncertainty.


### [446] [Multi-Sensitive Distributional Fairness: Joint Wasserstein Audits and Shapley Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19252)
*Agathe Fernandes Machado, Charlotte Jamotton, Arthur Charpentier*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Fairness audits with several sensitive attributes are often performed one coordinate at a time. This can miss purely intersectional disparities: a score may be independent of each sensitive coordinate while depending on their joint profile. We study distributional demographic parity for a real-valued score using one-dimensional Wasserstein geometry. We distinguish two complementary constructions. Conditional and sequential audits characterize joint parity through their zero sets, but their positive values do not generally decompose a fixed joint Wasserstein disparity. By contrast, full-cell conditional quantile functions define a Hilbert-valued random variable whose variance is the joint disparity. Conditional expectations of this quantile process induce a monotone information game: every reveal order yields an exact martingale decomposition of the same joint disparity, while Shapley values provide an order-free attribution across sensitive attributes. Under independent sensitive attributes, the game reduces to a Hoeffding-ANOVA decomposition and Shapley splits each interaction energy equally among its members; under dependence, orthogonality is lost and explicit formulas reveal suppression and redundancy effects that marginal audits can hide. We give fixed-grid estimators and permutation calibration, and illustrate the framework on synthetic examples and ACSIncome. The resulting framework separates three questions that are often conflated: whether joint distributional parity holds, how a fixed joint disparity decomposes along an information path, and how that disparity can be attributed across sensitive coordinates without privileging an ordering.


### [447] [State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19484)
*Ruihang Han, Li-Hsiang Lin*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Sequential conformal prediction needs to decide which past errors should calibrate uncertainty for the current target. Recency and online error feedback are useful when recent observations remain relevant, but the recent past can be a poor reference when comparable states recur at nonadjacent times, with the recurrence pattern generally unknown. We propose State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction (SMSCP), which constructs an explicit one-sided local-linear state from pre-target information, uses state similarity to select a matched reference set of past calibration scores, and then applies the ordinary unweighted conformal order statistic. In contrast to existing approaches, SMSCP uses state similarity to identify calibration observations relevant to the target score quantile. This motivates a theoretical analysis of whether nearby states have comparable target score quantiles under temporal dependence. We establish control of the resulting conformal cutoff and characterize predictive information not captured by the chosen state. Simulations show that state matching is most effective when score-relevant states recur across nonadjacent times, while revealing settings in which its advantage weakens or reverses. Across solar irradiance and bike-demand benchmarks, SMSCP improves interval score over recency, weighted, adaptive, and HopCPT comparators, and achieves coverage closer to the nominal 0.90 level than ResCP on five of six benchmarks.


### [448] [High-Probability Regret Bounds for Feel-Good Thompson Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19513)
*Xuheng Li, Qingyue Zhao, Quanquan Gu*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Feel-good Thompson sampling (FGTS) augments the posterior of vanilla Thompson sampling with an exploration term that favors reward functions with high predicted optimal values. This modification achieves minimax-optimal regret up to logarithmic factors in linear contextual bandits, but existing analyses of its two standard variants establish regret guarantees in expectation. In this paper, we derive the first high-probability regret bounds for both cumulative and current-step FGTS in contextual bandits with general realizable function approximation. Our bounds depend on the corresponding decoupling coefficients and the prior mass near the true reward function. In the $d$-dimensional linear setting, both variants satisfy $\mathrm{Regret}(T)=O\big(d\sqrt T\log(2T)+\sqrt T\log(1/\delta)\big)$ with probability at least $1-\delta$, where $T$ is the time horizon. This bound matches the dimension and horizon dependence of algorithms based on the upper confidence bounds, up to logarithmic factors. Relative to the expected-regret guarantees, the cost of controlling the regret with high probability is confined to additive terms with at most linear dependence on $\log(1/\delta)$. The proof ideas are partially developed by GPT-6 Astra under the authors' guidance.


### [449] [Noisy-Label Calibration with Multiple Unreliable Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19543)
*Keisuke Hanada, Futoshi Futami*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: In medical annotation and crowdsourcing, multiple annotators assign labels to each example, but these labels may be incorrect. Treating such noisy labels as ground truth can bias calibration evaluation. To address this problem, we propose methods for evaluating and recalibrating a fixed binary predictor under the underlying clean-label distribution, using only noisy annotations. First, given annotator-specific noise probabilities, we propose Noise Corrected ECE (NC-ECE), a binned estimator of expected calibration error (ECE) under class-conditional noise. We derive non-asymptotic bounds on the estimator's bias. Then, we propose practical algorithms for estimating unknown noise probabilities under conditional independence and label-orientation assumptions. We also develop loss-based and histogram post-hoc recalibration with clean-risk and calibration guarantees, respectively. Experiments on semi-synthetic benchmarks show that the proposed ECE estimator closely tracks its clean-label oracle counterpart and that the recalibration methods substantially improve latent-label calibration, whereas uncorrected recalibration against noisy annotations can degrade it.


### [450] [On Inherent Privacy of Posterior Sampling: A Unified Rényi-Divergence Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19573)
*Ruyu Zhou, Fang Liu*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Differential Privacy (DP) provides a rigorous framework for quantifying privacy guarantees. While most methods achieve DP by injecting calibrated random noise, the intrinsic randomness of certain procedures such as sampling can yield DP guarantees ``for free''. In this work, we develop a unified Rényi divergence framework to characterize the inherent DP guarantees of sampling from a Bayesian posterior distribution. We show that the privacy loss incurred by One-Posterior-Sampling (OPS) is governed by posterior exponential moments of the single-record log-likelihood ratio, and establish explicit DP guarantees under uniformly bounded, sub-Gaussian, or sub-exponential tail regimes. We apply the framework to representative Bayesian models, including categorical likelihoods with arbitrary priors, Gaussian-Gaussian conjugate pair, generalized linear models from the exponential family and linear regression with Gaussian likelihood, both with Gaussian priors on the regression coefficients. Our results elucidate sample size, model structure, neighboring relations in DP (bounded or unbounded), and how prior if applicable, jointly determine the inherent DP guarantees of OPS. Furthermore, our results recover the existing DP guarantees for OPS as special cases -- often with tighter privacy loss bounds -- and substantially broaden the class of Bayesian models for which the inherent DP guarantees of OPS can be rigorously characterized.


### [451] [Projected Wasserstein-Fisher-Rao Flows for Hawkes Background-Intensity Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19588)
*Isaias Banales, Arturo Jaramillo*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We consider the problem of estimating the background intensity of a Hawkes process when the triggering mechanism is known, treating the background as an unknown finite positive measure with a regular density. We propose a likelihood-based variational approach based on the Wasserstein-Fisher-Rao geometry. In this setting, the relevant first variations are naturally distribution-valued and therefore fall outside the classical gradient-flow formulation. We develop a distributional framework for the resulting dynamics and give them a finite-dimensional meaning through projections onto statistical manifolds. We identify the true background as the unique zero of a limiting generalized gradient and, under suitable stability, compactness, integrability and approximation conditions, establish a conditional consistency theorem for measurable estimators satisfying the empirical projected equations on events whose probabilities tend to one. Finally, we specialize the construction to Gaussian-mixture manifolds and obtain an explicit finite-dimensional scheme.


### [452] [Risk-Controlled DFT Selection for Machine-Learned Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19630)
*Kun Ryu, Yeo Jin Jung, Shyue Ping Ong, Claire Donnat, Ying Shirley Meng*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Automated materials discovery requires evaluating large pools of candidate configurations, with density functional theory (DFT) as a major throughput bottleneck. Pretrained machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) approximate DFT cheaply, but their errors vary by orders of magnitude across configurations, making it difficult to decide when to trust the MLIP predictions and when to fall back to DFT. We frame this decision as a risk-controlled selection problem and solve it within a conformal risk control (CRC) framework. Each configuration is mapped from atomic numbers and Cartesian coordinates to a fixed geometric descriptor, which a lightweight predictor uses to estimate an MLIP-DFT disagreement score in energy, average force, and maximum force. CRC then calibrates this score into a selection threshold for DFT labeling such that the MLIP error on the unselected configurations, which keep their MLIP labels, stays below a user-chosen fraction $\alpha$ of the expected error. Under an identical guarantee across eight dataset-MLIP pairs, our predictor routes 7% and 14% fewer configurations to DFT at $\alpha=0.3$ than committee baselines. Because committee disagreement overlooks errors the MLIPs share, our gains are largest when the DFT labels and MLIP training data differ in DFT functional and basis set, and narrow when the two agree. Without model access, the structure-only predictor also ranks errors as well as the target MLIP's latent representation and outperforms widely used descriptors such as Smooth Overlap of Atomic Positions (SOAP) and Atom-Centered Symmetry Functions (ACSF).


### [453] [SNACK: A Sequential Notation Framework for Probabilistic Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19781)
*Hohyun Kim, Hyesung Kim, Min-hwan Oh, Seunggeun Lee*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We introduce SNACK, a string representation for probabilistic graph generation that enables sequence models to generate and model graphs with discrete node and edge features. SNACK linearizes graphs by encoding the nonzero entries of the lower-triangular adjacency matrix, establishing a bijective correspondence between valid sequences and ordered adjacency matrices. This design enables invalid logit masking to enforce structural constraints--such as chemical valence and ordering rules--guaranteeing 100% valid generated sequences. SNACK also enables principled string-based GFlowNet training by explicitly accounting for node-ordering symmetries. Across standard graph-generation tasks and molecular distribution-learning benchmarks, SNACK achieves competitive sample quality, while substantially improving the throughput of GFlowNet training relative to graph-based generation. Probing analyses further show that models trained on SNACK encode meaningful local and global graph structure despite receiving only sequential supervision. These results position SNACK as a practical framework for probabilistic graph generation with deep sequence models.


### [454] [Calibration and Risk-Budgeted Decisions with System One Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19811)
*Soundouss Messoudi, Youness Mansar*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: LLM workflows and agents rely on a set of binary decisions like allow/block an input, or discard/retain a retrieved document. When human deferral is unavailable, uncertain cases must still receive an action. We study System One decision scores in this setting by defaulting to a conservative fallback when uncertain, and controlling the false omission risk within the subset of inputs assigned the utility-maximizing action. Across six datasets covering moderation, safety, and reranking, we compare general System One models with task baselines and a local open-weights fine-tuned System One model. We show that naive baselines for threshold selection exceed the target risk in roughly half of settings with defined test risk, compared with 7.8\% for Learn Then Test (LTT), which leverages finite-sample statistical guarantees. Our results show why relying on a probability-like interface is insufficient for deployment decisions and how risk-budgeted selection trades useful action against observed error.


### [455] [Text-Informed Training Data Filtering for Enhanced Virtual Flow Meter Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19860)
*Kristoffer Hjelmen Nesland, Bjarne Grimstad, Antorweep Chakravorty, Dan Sui*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: In sectors such as energy, aquaculture and chemical processing, the effectiveness of both mechanistic and data-driven models is frequently limited by poor-quality training data, which obscures underlying physical laws and degrades predictive accuracy. Such quality issues commonly manifest as anomalies in the dataset, ranging from sensor faults to process upsets and incorrect operator inputs. While traditional rule-based filtering can remove a subset of the anomalies, it is unable to leverage contextual information stored as unstructured text, such as engineer comments and operational logs that explain under what conditions the data was collected. We propose a text-informed data filtering method that leverages large language models (LLMs) to integrate unstructured operational data with tabular sensor data. The method consists of two steps: (1) contextual anomaly detection by reasoning over both data modalities, and (2) generation of a binary mask that is applied to filter the training dataset for model development. To investigate the potential benefit, a steady-state soft-sensor regression model, namely a virtual flow meter that estimates well flow rates, is used as the downstream evaluation target. Experiments on a synthetic oil and gas dataset show improved model performance compared to traditional rule-based filtering. Ablation studies show that textual operator comments can provide filtering-relevant signals beyond sensor data alone, with the multimodal approach reducing downstream MAPE from 7.1% to 5.4% relative to sensor-only filtering. This work demonstrates that integrating unstructured operational knowledge with sensor data through LLMs can improve data quality for industrial machine learning applications, offering a scalable alternative to manual data curation.


### [456] [On the Optimisation Landscape of Overparameterised Weighted MMD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19861)
*Tharrshann Jayan Logarajah, Jeremias Knoblauch, Louis Sharrock*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the optimisation landscape of the maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) over weighted atomic measures. For the one-dimensional Gaussian kernel, we show that, for any compactly supported target distribution, sufficiently many particles ensure that every local minimum has arbitrarily small MMD. Moreover, any stationary point whose MMD remains bounded away from zero is unstable under an unweighted gradient flow over the particle locations. These results hold both when the weights are constrained to the simplex, and when they are unconstrained but optimised at each step. When the target is itself finitely supported, we further strengthen this result: beyond an explicit particle threshold, there are no spurious local minima, and every spurious stationary point is unstable. To our knowledge, these are the first finite-particle overparameterisation guarantees of their kind for weighted MMD optimisation.


### [457] [Convergence Rates of Gaussian Process Thompson Sampling for Efficient Global Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19925)
*Haowei Wang, Yilin Zheng*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Efficient global optimization aims to identify a global optimum of an expensive black-box function within a limited evaluation budget $N$. We investigate the convergence rates of Gaussian process Thompson sampling (GP-TS) under noiseless observations, assuming a fixed objective function in a reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS). For Matérn kernels with smoothness parameter $\nu$ in dimension $d$, we establish a worst-case expected simple-regret upper bound of $O(N^{-\nu/d}\sqrt{\log N})$ for both discrete GP-TS on sufficiently fine grids and its continuous counterpart. For the continuous implementation, we develop a nested-grid argument to control posterior sample paths over the entire domain. We further establish matching lower bounds of $\Omega(N^{-5/2}\sqrt{\log N})$ for both implementations under a fixed one-dimensional Matérn-$5/2$ kernel, demonstrating that the logarithmic penalty is intrinsic to standard GP-TS in this setting. Motivated by this limitation, we propose Gaussian Process Capped Thompson Sampling (GP-CTS), which caps posterior sample paths using a valid RKHS norm upper bound while preserving optimism. This modification successfully eliminates the logarithmic penalty and achieves the minimax-optimal rate $\Theta(N^{-\nu/d})$ for both discrete and continuous implementations.


### [458] [Minimax estimation of the pseudo-spectral gap of continuous Markov chains, with applications to empirical concentration bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20007)
*Vahe Karagulyan, Mikołaj Kasprzak, Pierre Alquier*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the minimax estimation of the pseudo-spectral gap $\gamma_{ps}$ of a Markov chain from the observation of a single trajectory. Extending earlier works on finite-state space chains by Wolfer and Kontorovich (2024), we provide an estimator in the case of a compact, continuous state-space. Our estimator appears to be minimax-optimal on the class of chains with a transition operator with Lipschitz density, up to logarithmic terms. Our estimator, combined with Paulin's version of Bernstein inequality, allows to derive empirical concentration inequalities for Markov chains. This unlocks many applications, from confidence intervals, to active learning and generalization bounds. In particular, for rested bandits with Markov rewards, we provide a version of UCB with a regret bound similar to the one in the iid case, without assuming any knowledge on the pseudo-spectral gap of the chains.


### [459] [Weighted Adjoint Matching: Finetuning Flow Matching Models with Time-Dependent Control Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20035)
*Eliot Beyler, Francis Bach*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We introduce Weighted Adjoint Matching, a method to finetune a pretrained diffusion or flow matching model given a differentiable reward. We derive it from Adjoint Matching, which reformulates finetuning as a stochastic optimal control problem with a quadratic control cost. Building on the observation that these models generate coarse features before fine details, we place a time-dependent weight on the control cost. A high cost early in the generative process preserves balance and diversity in the coarse features, which carry most of the semantics, while a low cost at the end lets the model increase the reward by refining details. We prove that our method is equivalent to maximizing the reward under time-varying Kullback--Leibler regularization on the generative paths. Experiments on Gaussian mixtures and on MNIST show a better tradeoff between reward and mode balance than Adjoint Matching.


### [460] [Mixed Poisson diffusion models and application to posterior sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20218)
*Thibaut Pellerin, Alain Durmus, Elinor Berger, Eric Barat*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Diffusion models are a powerful framework for learning complex probability distributions. While widely used diffusion models for continuous data rely on Gaussian noising processes, we instead develop diffusion models based on Poisson noise. More precisely, we introduce and study a new mathematical framework, called Mixed Poisson Diffusion Models (MPDM), with noising dynamics driven by multivariate counting processes. Our methodology is motivated by Poisson inverse problems. The success of Gaussian diffusion models has led to their use as prior models in a growing number of posterior sampling methods for inverse problems. However, most of these approaches focus on Gaussian inverse problems, in which observations are generated through a deterministic forward operator corrupted by additive Gaussian noise. By contrast, Poisson inverse problems, where observations are discrete and Poisson distributed, remain comparatively underexplored in this literature. Building on our Poisson generative dynamics, we develop a natural framework for posterior sampling in Poisson inverse problems and derive several posterior sampling algorithms based on the proposed model. We evaluate our approach on realistic Poisson inverse problems and show that the resulting framework is well suited to this class of problems.


### [461] [The minimal width of neural networks and immersions of real projective space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20229)
*BaoZhi Chu, Ben Weinkove*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We investigate the minimal width of deep neural networks that can approximate all continuous functions from $\mathbb{R}^n$ to $\mathbb{R}^m$ in the topology of uniform convergence on compact subsets. We show that whenever the real projective space $\mathbb{RP}^{n-1}$ admits a smooth immersion into $\mathbb{R}^n$, the minimal width is at least $n+\min\{n,m\}$, assuming that the activation function is continuous and monotone. As a consequence, the minimal width of ReLU networks is $n+m$ whenever $n$ is equal to 2, 3, 4, 7 or 8 and $1\le m \le n$, extending results of Hanin-Sellke and Hwang. This suggests an intriguing connection between the approximation theory of neural networks and differential topology.


### [462] [Posterior Frequencies and the Cost of Not Fitting: A Correction to a Memorization Lower Bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20402)
*Zhenisbek Assylbekov, Rustem Takhanov*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Feldman's memorization lower bound assigns a frequency coefficient based on how often a point appears in the training sample. We give an exact three-point counterexample to its conditional and averaged forms, using independent fair labels and a permutation-equivariant learner. A sequence of sampling priors makes the ratio of the proposed penalty to actual excess risk grow exponentially, ruling out a universal positive constant-factor repair. The priors vary with sample size, the offending profiles become rare, and both quantities vanish. Retaining the original model, we obtain an exact replacement using full-sample posterior probabilities. Under exchangeability, these depend on multiplicity and the complete frequency profile. We characterize the largest multiplicity-only coefficients valid for unrestricted learners. The original coefficient has the posterior-mean form of Good's exact frequency formula and is an occupancy-weighted average of the corrected coefficients.
