# cs.SE [[Back]](#toc)

### [463] [JevCoder: When Does Decision Delegation Pay Off in Code Repair?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19596)
*Jiahao Zhang, Yifan Zhang, Yu Huang*

Main category: cs.SE

Abstract: A coding agent repeatedly makes decisions as it repairs code. These decisions shape repair trajectories and affect the token cost of successive model requests. We ask which repair decisions can be delegated to a low-cost probabilistic judge to reduce token use while maintaining repair quality. The judges answer questions with probabilities over predefined answers, without generating explanations. We evaluate judgment quality across repair stages and test how individual interventions change subsequent repair work. We compare these interventions with unguided repair, which uses no judge, and with the same workflow using the repair model itself as the judge. The benefit depends on the work an intervention changes, rather than the cost of the judgment alone. Based on these studies, we propose JevCoder, a workflow built around Jev, a low-cost probabilistic judge. It combines code-snippet recommendations with action-type guidance and uses judgments to determine when to submit. We evaluate three repair models, each on the same 400 tasks: 200 from SWE-bench Verified and 200 from SWE-Bench Pro Verified. Compared to unguided repair, JevCoder reduces mean repair-model token use by 21.6%-50.6%, with higher observed repair rates across all three models.


### [464] [When Does the Dependency Graph Help Code-to-Architecture Mapping?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19769)
*Rakhshanda Jabeen, Morgan Ericsson, Jonas Nordqvist, Anna Wingkvist*

Main category: cs.SE

Abstract: Architectural technical debt arises when a system's implementation drifts from its inten ded modular decomposition, while the mapping between source files and architectural modu les is missing, incomplete, or costly to maintain manually. We formulate code-to-archite cture mapping as semi-supervised node classification over a directed multi-relational de pendency graph, with files as nodes, typed dependencies as edges, and lexical features d erived from code identifiers and directory layout. Starting from a small labeled sample, an iterative self-training procedure expands the mapping to the remaining files. We com pare a graph convolutional network (GCN), a graph attention network (GAT), and a graph-f ree multi-layer perceptron (MLP), using identical features and training procedures for t he learned models, against a Naive Bayes reference. Across 100 runs on ten open-source J ava and C/C++ systems, all three neural models outperform the reference on both F1 metri cs. GAT achieves the highest averages of system-level median macro and micro F1, at 83.5 and 92.2, respectively, whereas MLP remains competitive or superior on several systems. On Chrome, the largest system with 18,343 files, GAT and GCN exceed MLP by 22 and 19 ma cro-F1 points, respectively, and have substantially fewer persistently misclassified fil es. Connectivity and homophily are associated with where neural message passing helps, w hile multi-relational aggregation outperforms a single merged edge type on every system for both GCN and GAT. A follow-up study finds that several model rankings depend on the training seed, qualifying smaller advantages without removing the large graph benefit ob served on Chrome.


### [465] [Doc2Harness: Benchmarking LLMs on Long-Horizon Harness Engineering of Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20344)
*Yuyao Ge, Shenghua Liu, Peizhong Gao, Chendi Ge, Chenxu Liu, Yiwei Wang, Lingrui Mei, Baolong Bi, Jiayu Yao, Jiafeng Guo, Xueqi Cheng*

Main category: cs.SE

Abstract: Agents driven by large language models are increasingly deployed on long-horizon tasks, where they use tools and modify real workspaces. How well they perform depends as much on the harness around the model as on the model itself. Existing benchmarks measure downstream task success under a fixed harness, leaving harness engineering itself unexplored. We propose Doc2Harness, a two-stage benchmark that evaluates long-horizon harness engineering directly. In Stage 1, a build model operating inside a fixed builder harness receives an interface-only contract skeleton and a development document that consolidates behavior across twelve frontier harnesses, and then constructs a complete candidate harness in an isolated container. In Stage 2, the frozen candidate harness runs in containers separate from the construction environment, one per test case, and is driven end to end by a scripted mock model. We score each test case using four observable channels, namely the requests sent to the model, the files on disk, the process exit code, and the command output; positive controls rule out vacuous passes. Across nineteen frontier models, each evaluated with three independent builds on 328 test cases, GPT-6 Astra achieves the highest pass rate at 53.05%, while the weakest model reaches 27.44%, a gap of roughly twenty-six percentage points. Even the strongest model fails nearly half of the test cases, and failures cluster around how cross-cutting functionality is split across modules. To our knowledge, Doc2Harness provides the first deterministic benchmark for measuring long-horizon harness engineering.


### [466] [SWE-Stream: Scaling Executable Training Data for SWE Agents via Progressive Construction and Elastic Streaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20193)
*Chengyang Liu, Wen Yang, Shifeng Chen, Delai Qiu, Ji Yang, Junnan Zhu, Yining Wang, Shengping Liu*

Main category: cs.SE

Abstract: Software engineering agents have advanced rapidly, but scaling their training remains constrained by the limited availability of high-quality executable tasks. Development histories contain valuable signals for constructing such tasks, yet they often require substantial reconstruction due to incomplete environments, missing tests, and inconsistent artifacts. We present SWE-Stream, a system for scaling executable SWE training data through progressive construction and elastic streaming execution. SWE-Stream progressively validates and refines task candidates, reusing existing artifacts when possible and applying targeted completion only when necessary. An elastic scheduler asynchronously coordinates construction stages under shared resource constraints, adapting execution budgets based on observed workload demands. Using development histories from 10,728 repositories across six programming languages, SWE-Stream constructs 80,874 validated tasks and collects 118,688 successful trajectories. Fine-tuning Qwen3-30B-A3B-Thinking-2507 on these trajectories achieves resolve rates of 69.0%, 49.3%, and 45.42% on SWE-bench Verified, Multilingual, and Pro, respectively. In a controlled comparison with approximately matched training budgets, SWE-Stream achieves a 45.6% resolve rate on SWE-bench Verified, outperforming three existing data sources. Moreover, its streaming construction strategy improves efficiency, delivering $1.45\times$ the accepted-task throughput of batch construction and accepting 13.2% more tasks than fixed-limit streaming.


### [467] [SWE-Nexus: Automated Composition of Software Engineering Tasks from Test Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20038)
*Songwei Yu, Ziqi Miao, Jingxuan Xu, Junhao Fan, Haoyang Huang, Yuan Xiong, Zihang Xu, Weihao Li, Wenqiang Zhu, Yuechen Luo, Xinshun Feng, Yanan Wu, Ken Deng*

Main category: cs.SE

Abstract: Historical code changes provide convenient task boundaries, but do not identify which production functions selected tests exercise or support composing validated behaviors beyond a single change. We present SWE-Nexus, a fully automated pipeline that constructs repository-level implementation tasks from test execution. Four construction agents select tests, adapt tracing and evaluation protocols to each repository, and generate behavioral specifications. For each selected test file, tracing records an observed execution footprint of production function bodies. A scope is retained only if all expected scoring points pass in the original repository and masking the traced bodies causes at least one failure or error. Compatible scopes are composed deterministically, with shared functions counted once and every constituent test file revalidated under the complete combined mask. The benchmark contains 600 tasks from 105 repositories across six programming languages, including 116 atomic and 484 composite tasks. We evaluate 12 models under three agent scaffolds. The best configuration resolves 37.8% of tasks in one attempt, and scope-weighted scoring measures partial recovery. SWE-Nexus enables task construction from validated behavior beyond individual historical changes.


<div id='cs.LO'></div>

# cs.LO [[Back]](#toc)

### [468] [Beyond LLM Judge: Mechanical Verification for Agentic Autoformalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20573)
*Anna Mei, Michael R. Douglas*

Main category: cs.LO

Abstract: We introduce a novel method for verifying Lean formalizations that goes beyond LLM-judged faithfulness. Unlike most verification methods, which rely on LLMs or trained models, our method combines LLM-based semantic review with mechanical numerical and vacuity checks, providing checkable feedback and detecting errors missed by LLM judges. We apply this method, along with our Extract-Verify-Proof autoformalization pipeline, to construct the Formalized Library of Mathematical Functions (FLMF), a partial formalization of the NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions (DLMF). On three labeled datasets, our method achieves the lowest accepted-error rate among the evaluated methods, measured as the fraction of incorrect statements accepted by the verification method. Expert review of the results also identifies translation and source defects in existing datasets. Code available at https://github.com/annamei1/FLMF-Ch7ErrFunc-Release.git.


<div id='physics.chem-ph'></div>

# physics.chem-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [469] [TorchSpin: Differentiable Electron Paramagnetic Resonance Simulation in PyTorch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18924)
*Alec H. Follmer*

Main category: physics.chem-ph

Abstract: Interpreting electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) spectra commonly requires repeated forward simulation and optimization of spin Hamiltonian parameters. Established software provides mature simulation and fitting methods, but these calculations are not generally available as differentiable components within machine-learning workflows. We present TorchSpin, an open-source PyTorch framework that ports the organization and numerical conventions of EasySpin into a tensor-based computational environment. Representative calculations spanning continuous-wave EPR, ENDOR, pulse EPR, spin dynamics, trajectory simulation, and magnetometry closely reproduced corresponding EasySpin results. In matched benchmarks, TorchSpin CPU execution was faster than EasySpin for six of nine workloads although performance depended strongly on the calculation. We therefore focused on the capabilities enabled by automatic differentiation in PyTorch. In a Cu(II) powder-spectrum demonstration, feedback propagated through TorchSpin improved the quality of a supervised convolutional neural network (CNN) without using parameter labels during refinement. Models trained from randomly initialized CNNs using only noisy spectra and TorchSpin reconstruction loss achieved median similarities of $\geq$ 0.99. CNNs trained on TorchSpin-generated libraries also transferred without adaptation to independently generated EasySpin spectra with performance comparable to EasySpin-trained controls. Once trained, the CNN produced initial parameter estimates in approximately 1 ms per spectrum while a batch of 512 was processed in approximately 1.3 ms with GPU acceleration. TorchSpin therefore provides both a tested Python implementation of established EPR calculations and a differentiable physical layer for rapid learned inference, spectral refinement, and parameter-label-free training.


<div id='cs.PL'></div>

# cs.PL [[Back]](#toc)

### [470] [Recursive Formation and First-Class Reuse of Compositional Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19414)
*Hang Lin*

Main category: cs.PL

Abstract: We introduce FormCraft, a framework for representing acquired operations as first-class capabilities that can be recursively composed, directly invoked, and selectively retained in a persistent library through domain-specific verification. The framework separates capability well-formedness and public callability from later admission to persistent storage while preserving formation provenance and operational identity. We formalize recursive compositional closure over a uniform capability space and show that formed composites can be reused through the same public interface without re-forming their historical derivation. Experiments across continuous, discrete, propositional, and sequence domains demonstrate instance-independent reuse, independent checking, persistent acquisition, and reduced reuse-time search work under first-class exposure.


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [471] [Federated Unlearning of Quantum Data via Classical Shadows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19077)
*Zihao Ding, Jun Huang, Cong-cong Xing*

Main category: quant-ph

Abstract: Quantum federated learning trains a quantum neural network (QNN) across nodes that hold quantum data, such as the states of quantum sensors. To forget data, unlearning methods for quantum models replay them, and federated unlearning from stored updates removes whole nodes only. Quantum data cannot be replayed, since a state is consumed when measured and cannot be cloned. We propose Shadow-based Data Erasure (SHADE), in which each node records a classical shadow of every training state, a few randomized single-qubit measurements taken at training time. The gradient and curvature of a QNN on a state are expectations of observables, so SHADE estimates them for the forget data from the shadows at any later parameters and removes their effect by a federated Newton correction. Only classical estimates cross the network. We prove that the shadow size needed for a target unlearning error grows exponentially in the size of the backward light cone of the measured observable and not in the number of qubits. In simulation with 50 nodes and on edge boards, SHADE matches the forget accuracy of retraining within 0.5 points, without replay and in under 0.2 s.


<div id='cs.CG'></div>

# cs.CG [[Back]](#toc)

### [472] [Geometric Kernel Diversity: An Axiomatic Diversity Measure in Euclidean Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19570)
*Ditbul Ban, Younghun Roh, Julian Shun, Vihan Lakshman*

Main category: cs.CG

Abstract: Quantifying the diversity of a set of objects is a fundamental problem in machine learning and statistics. Mironov and Prokhorenkova (2025) recently showed that three axioms-monotonicity, uniqueness, and continuity-are crucial for a reliable diversity measure and that none of the standard metrics in the literature satisfy all three. They also constructed two measures that do satisfy the axioms, but both are NP-hard to compute, leaving open the question of whether efficient axiomatic diversity measures can exist. We study a Euclidean-restricted version of this question. We introduce Geometric Kernel Diversity (GKD), which associates a radial kernel solid with each point and measures the volume of their union. We show that canonical GKD satisfies all three Mironov-Prokhorenkova axioms on Euclidean-realizable inputs. For canonical GKD, we give exact polynomial-time algorithms in dimensions $d \le 3$. In arbitrary dimensions, we give a randomized algorithm that returns a $(1 \pm \varepsilon)$-approximation with probability at least $1-\delta$ in time $O \left(\frac{nd}{\varepsilon^2}\log\frac{2}{\delta}\right)$ for $n$ points. The axiomatic guarantee applies to exact canonical GKD; the randomized algorithm approximates this deterministic quantity. We further show that the two diversity measures of Mironov and Prokhorenkova (2025) remain NP-hard on Euclidean-realizable inputs and, unless $\mathrm{NP} = \mathrm{RP}$, admit no FPRAS even on this restricted domain.


<div id='stat.CO'></div>

# stat.CO [[Back]](#toc)

### [473] [Edgewise Envelopes Between Balanced Forman and Ollivier-Ricci Curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13535)
*Giorgio Micaletto, Tebe Nigrelli*

Main category: stat.CO

Abstract: Evaluating Ollivier-Ricci (OR) curvature on large-scale graphs is computationally prohibitive due to the necessity of solving an optimal transport problem for every edge. We bypass this bottleneck by deriving explicit, two-sided, piecewise-affine transfer moduli between the transport-based OR curvature and the combinatorial Balanced Forman (BF) curvature. We establish deterministic bounds for $\mathfrak{c}_{\rm OR}(i,j)$ parameterized by 2-hop local graph combinatorics, reducing the edgewise evaluation complexity from an optimal transport linear program to a worst-case $\mathcal{O}\left(\max_{v \in V} \operatorname{deg}(v)^{2.5}\right)$ time, entirely eliminating the reliance on global solvers. Empirical scalability benchmarks confirm these theoretical guarantees, demonstrating that the proposed transfer moduli yield significant asymptotic and constant-factor speedups over the steep polynomial scaling of exact OR evaluation. Furthermore, the tightness of these bounds is validated via distributional analyses on canonical random graphs and empirical networks, with the derived analytical bands enclosing the empirical distributions independent of degree heterogeneity, geometry, or clustering, providing a scalable, computationally efficient framework for rigorous statistical network analysis.


<div id='cs.IR'></div>

# cs.IR [[Back]](#toc)

### [474] [Revisiting Generative vs. Dense Retrieval: A Static Value Prior Reverses the Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19065)
*Renbo Tu, Yang Liu, Shuying Sun*

Main category: cs.IR

Abstract: Generative retrieval (GR) has emerged as a strong candidate generator for sequential recommendation as language models and semantic-item identifiers have improved. However, GR is not a uniformly stronger replacement of dense retrieval (DR) in online retail candidate generation. We examine their differences on two Amazon Toys datasets under different metrics. GR leads on best relevant item value among users both methods cover, while DR leads on exact-target recall and relevant value across the ranked list. With deep analysis, we find that popularity contributes the most to GR's measured value lead. Adding the same static item value to DR in one reranking step reverses this lead and keeps recall above GR. Finally, on an industrial-scale internal e-commerce dataset with LLM-judged relevance, we show that a 14B SID-LLM still underperforms a $47\times$ smaller dense encoder.


### [475] [Bridging the Topological Divide: Dual-Branch Graph Distillation with Semantic Priors for Robust Cold-Start Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19578)
*Yuanfeng Qiu*

Main category: cs.IR

Abstract: Graph Collaborative Filtering models have achieved remarkable success in recommendation systems. However, they inherently suffer from a catastrophic representation collapse in strict cold-start scenarios due to the topological isolation of unobserved items. While integrating multi-modal semantic features offers a potential remedy, directly mapping isolated semantic attributes into a globally topology-driven collaborative space frequently precipitates representation degradation and optimization conflicts. To bridge this fundamental structural divide, we propose DualGCN, an innovative Dual-Branch Graph Knowledge Distillation Framework. Operating under a decoupled two-stage training paradigm, DualGCN first constructs a robust collaborative teacher network where item representations are anchored by a topology-smoothed semantic prior, effectively reshaping the latent manifold. Subsequently, a semantic student branch employs a residual mapping mechanism and a magnitude-agnostic cosine distillation objective to achieve high-fidelity cross-modal alignment without disrupting established collaborative signals. Furthermore, we introduce a dynamic norm reconstruction mechanism to rigorously calibrate the physical scales of novel and warm items during global retrieval. Extensive experiments across three real-world benchmark datasets demonstrate that DualGCN significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in strict cold-start scenarios while comprehensively preserving recommendation accuracy for highly active items.


<div id='physics.soc-ph'></div>

# physics.soc-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [476] [Popularity Trajectories and Mixing Patterns of Urban Venues in Two European Cities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18917)
*Wang Ngai Yeung, Lakmal Meegahapola, Daniele Quercia, Riccardo Di Clemente*

Main category: physics.soc-ph

Abstract: Urban venues such as restaurants, shops, bars and parks host the daily life of a city, and their collective rise and fall signals how vibrant a neighbourhood is. The usual assumption is that a venue's fortunes follow its neighbourhood's, so that how wealthy an area is and how many people live there will decide a venue's performance. Yet whether there is a common performance trajectory shared by venues over time is underexplored. In this study, we use 5.5 years of Foursquare data for about 10^4 venues in London and Barcelona. We found six distinguishable shapes recur in both cities, that are (1) Fall, (2) Rise, (3) Rise & Fall, (4) Steady, (5) Ephemeral (an early hit that loses popularity fast) and (6) Underdogs (a slow starter that gains popularity late). However, no recovery shape appears, that is neither city yields a U-shaped fall-and-rise group. Additionally, we found that deprivation and population density are not indicative which shape a venue follows. We also discovered that how early a shape can be recognized depends on when its distinctive segment occurs, not a shape's complexity. Proximity between venues, however, does predict how well-performing a venue is. We uncovered a mixing pattern where venues of the same shape tend to locate together more often than chance within roughly 200 to 600 metres. Finally, our qualitative reading of two contrasting districts, one rising in London and one falling in Barcelona, show that comparable socioeconomic conditions but divergent gentrification histories can generate vastly different popularity trajectories. All in all, our results altogether point at that urban vibrancy emerges at the block- and street- level, and social and historical contexts matter in terms of the characteristics of such patterns.


<div id='cs.RO'></div>

# cs.RO [[Back]](#toc)

### [477] [DexMimic: A Generalist Dexterous Hand-Object Tracking Policy via Value-Guided Reset Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19089)
*Zhexi Luo, Zhixuan Xu, Rui Yang, Zeyu Xiao, Yiwen Hou, Lin Shao*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Achieving human-like dexterous manipulation remains a central challenge in robotics. Learning from human hand-object interaction (HOI) datasets offers a promising pathway to diverse dexterous skills, yet existing physics-based approaches train specialist policies for individual trajectories, thereby limiting skill reuse at scale. We introduce DexMimic, a generalist dexterous hand-object tracking policy jointly trained across diverse HOI trajectories. Efficient joint training requires allocating each rollout to both an undertrained phase and a starting state that supports continued tracking. DexMimic realizes this insight through Value-Guided Reset Allocation, which adapts the initialization distribution online to prioritize difficult phases and select reset states with higher expected tracking returns. We further introduce a benchmark spanning training, fine-tuning, and generalization. Experiments show improved tracking success and training efficiency, faster fine-tuning, and the potential for zero-shot generalization to unseen trajectories and objects; larger training sets further improve efficiency and generalization. Finally, we validate DexMimic on a real-world dexterous manipulation platform.


### [478] [Learning in Orbit: Onboard Training and Closed-Loop Attitude Control with a Thermal Segmentation Model on a Spacecraft](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19122)
*Elena Sorina Lupu, Hannah Grauer, Phaedrus Leeds, Nikhil Ranganathan, Connor Lee, Sulekha Kishore, Cailyn Smith, Shon Cortes, Brian Hardy, Benjamen Bycroft, Darren W. Rowen, Soon-Jo Chung*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Deep space missions increasingly require spacecraft capable of autonomously operating in environments with communication delays and bandwidth limitations. A key enabler of such autonomy is the ability to run learning-based algorithms directly onboard. In this work, we present a flight deployment on a 6U CubeSat that demonstrates (1) onboard training and inference of a thermal segmentation model on an NVIDIA Jetson TX2 NX GPU in space and (2) closed-loop attitude control using the segmentation model on a real-time, thermal image feed to track the Moon. In addition to this demonstration, we implement ROS 2 Foxy as a data transport middleware and integrate an open-source file system that provides strong data integrity guarantees and self-healing features. Over the course of approximately one year, we performed 12 experiments on the spacecraft, advancing the state of the art in autonomous spacecraft systems by showing how modern learning-based pipelines, commercially available hardware, and open-source software can operate reliably under the compute and environmental constraints of Low Earth Orbit (LEO).


### [479] [MIND: Instruction-Conditioned Incremental Grounding for Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19262)
*Mohamed Abouagour, Junhyun Nam, Wonse Jo, Byung-Cheol Min*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Robots following persistent language instructions must decide which cached room judgments remain valid as their environment changes. We present MIND (Maintenance via INstruction-conditioned Dependencies), a framework for maintaining target-room decisions without re-evaluating every room after each event. A language model compiles each instruction into dependencies for semantic qualification and target selection. The controller intersects semantic dependencies with event footprints, refreshes affected judgments, and updates reachability and selection separately. For fixed deterministic evaluation, planning, and selection, conservative dependency and footprint coverage preserves the decisions of full recomputation. On 240 five-event ProcTHOR sequences with an exact benchmark predicate, MIND reduces mean semantic evaluations from 30.6 to 11.2 while preserving all 1,200 post-event decisions. Frozen-evaluator experiments with Phi-4 and Qwen3-14B also preserve all 1,200 decisions, without improving the evaluators' underlying task accuracy. In 32 closed-loop AI2-THOR episodes using exact-predicate qualification and metadata-derived footprints, MIND matches the final target decision of full recomputation in every episode. Unitree G1 experiments demonstrate target reuse and reselection across room transitions without additional semantic evaluations under MIND, and abstention after a barrier and an object removal at 4 evaluations against 15 to 18. Additional materials are available at this https URL .


### [480] [From Failures to Learnable Worlds: Policy-Conditioned Environment Generation for Navigation Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19420)
*Yue Zhang, Han Lin, Zun Wang, Yidong Huang, Zaid Khan, Mohit Bansal*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Simulated environments enable scalable training data generation for embodied agents, but more data does not necessarily provide what a particular agent needs to learn. Existing environment-generation pipelines largely optimize generic properties such as realism, diversity, or task coverage, while different pretrained policies can fail for different environmental reasons. We therefore introduce WEDGE, a framework for weakness-guided navigation learning in which the failure modes of a pretrained agent guide the generation of training environments at each adaptation round. Given a pretrained policy, WEDGE uses an LLM to propose executable weakness features from contrasting successful and failed rollouts, automatically validates them on the diagnostic set, and selects the strongest validated weakness based on its success-rate gap. The validated weaknesses are then mapped to the environmental factors that control them. WEDGE varies these factors to construct environments in the difficulty regimes where the policy struggles, producing targeted training experience for adaptation. Across three navigation agents with different adaptation mechanisms, we find that policies exhibit distinct weakness profiles and benefit from different training environments. Weakness-conditioned adaptation consistently outperforms matched random and hard-example alternatives on ProcTHOR, while the resulting gains for Uni-NaVid and Gemini also extend to HM3D after ProcTHOR adaptation. Iterative re-diagnosis further shows that dominant weaknesses can persist or shift as the policy improves, providing updated guidance for subsequent environment construction. Together, these results show that policy failures are not only diagnostic signals, but actionable guidance for constructing training environments that target a policy's current weaknesses.


### [481] [IMU-Bench: Benchmarking Wrist IMU Representations Across Kinematic, Semantic and Clinical Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19481)
*Yiquan Li, Aidan Bradshaw, Chenfeng Gao, Karan Ahuja*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Wrist-worn inertial measurement units (IMUs) provide a common sensing modality for tasks ranging from activity and gesture recognition to pose estimation, inertial odometry and clinical motor assessment. Existing wearable benchmarks evaluate performance across multiple tasks, but leave open how supervision for one task affects transfer to others and whether these tasks benefit from a shared representation. We present IMU-Bench, a benchmark of ten tasks from eight datasets for studying IMU multi-task performance and cross-task transfer. We compare six self-supervised methods pretrained on unlabeled recorded and simulated wrist motion, evaluating classification, pose and velocity estimation, and IMU-language retrieval. We find that our masked autoencoder, InrtlWrist, achieves the best or joint-best frozen-probe performance on nine of ten tasks, and fine-tuning improves on training from scratch on nine of ten tasks, including full-body pose, odometry and retrieval. Across transfer and joint-training experiments, supervision that benefits one target can reduce performance on others, with supervised sources transferring within kinematic estimation while, under frozen probing, none approaches the pretrained encoder on language retrieval, and joint-training gains that come at a cost to the paired task. These findings distinguish broad downstream usefulness from complementary task supervision. Code, task cards and a leaderboard are publicly available.


### [482] [Instruction-Conditioned Motion Control for Robotic Arms: Language-to-Code Decomposition and Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19617)
*Fan Huang, Dezhong Tong, Changyu Chen, Haewoon Kwak, Jisun An*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Instruction-Conditioned Motion Control (ICMC) separates parameter specification, instruction generation, and code synthesis for language-driven robot control, making execution outcomes traceable to instructions and programs. We compare one-shot generation with ICMC under o1 and o4-mini on cube pick-and-place and letter-reshaping tasks for a UR5e arm in MuJoCo. We then train an open-weight instruction policy by GRPO through a frozen code generator on cube placement onto two- and five-cube letter layouts, averaging the rewards of five programs per instruction. The logged positive-reward rate rose from 31.6% to 35.3% (253/800 to 282/800) between the first and last five training steps. A log audit identifies positive binary labels associated with geometric-failure messages: at least 19 in that run, 18 in the dense-reward run, and 5 in the run with one program per instruction. In one case, all five rewards were 1.0 while the log reports a 0.9515 m miss against a 0.05 m tolerance; the verdict came from the program's own output stream. We distinguish logged reward rates from independently verified task success and report descriptive results for each configuration's single training trajectory. We introduce an independent evaluation protocol that checks task completion against fixed specifications, separating robot control from success assessment to support reproducible evaluation.


### [483] [Eye, Robot: Learning Expressive Gaze Dynamics via a VLM-Guided Transformer-VAE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19635)
*Yuhang Hu, Junlin Lyu, Yuan Fang, Xiaoying Shen, Lechen Zhang, Jiong Lin, Judah Goldfeder, Steven Tian, Hod Lipson*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Gaze coordinates attention in human interaction through coupled eye, head, and eyelid motion. A humanoid robot must choose where to look while generating a suitable movement toward that target; fixed tracking rules alone do not model the temporal structure of human gaze. We separate these tasks with an asynchronous vision-language model that selects a scene referent, a reactive perception module that maintains fixation, and a Realistic Gaze Transformer (RGT) that generates target-conditioned motion. RGT is a Transformer-VAE trained on markerless human recordings retargeted to the robot's 11-DoF joint space: self-supervised pretraining encodes recent full-head history, and autoregressive fine-tuning exposes the model to its own predictions during rollout. At inference, a four-dimensional binocular-eye trajectory conditions coordinated neck and eye motion; an auxiliary blink predictor triggers eyelid templates during playback. On a two-second benchmark in which all controllers receive the same ground-truth target, RGT-Base obtains the lowest reported overall seven-joint MSE, and physical demonstrations show the integrated targeting and control pipeline. The evidence establishes robot-space kinematic fidelity and real-time RGT inference; perceived naturalness and open-vocabulary target-selection accuracy remain questions for controlled evaluation.


### [484] [Uncertainty-Driven Self-Play for Robust Multi-Agent Driving Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19714)
*Sajad Marvi, Julian Schmidt, Alessandro Canevaro, Julian Jordan, Abhinav Valada*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Learning robust driving policies that generalize beyond their training domain remains a central challenge in motion planning. We propose an uncertainty-driven self-play scheme, in which half of the agents are rewarded for increasing their neighbors' epistemic uncertainty, while the other half learn to drive safely as the exploring agents continuously seek out their weaknesses. Epistemic uncertainty indicates where the policy is least confident and serves as an automatic proxy for scenario difficulty. Exploring agents use this signal to seek out hard cases, generating a challenging curriculum without manual scenario design. The resulting policy outperforms standard self-play, achieving best-in-class zero-shot transfer to nuplan Val14 (99.12% vs. 97.97%) and Argoverse 2 (82.39% vs. 78.03%) without fine-tuning, while also improving performance over standard self-play on Waymo. Our diversity-driven approach achieves 85% higher LSTM hidden-state coverage than the baseline, indicating a much richer set of behaviors. Further analysis shows that epistemic uncertainty serves as a calibrated difficulty detector, with highest-uncertain agents exhibiting 14.8 times the combined failure rate across collision, off-road, and DNF, of lowest-uncertain agents, enabling automatic prioritization of challenging scenarios without ground-truth labels.


### [485] [FoveaVLA: Foveation Actions Replace the Wrist Camera in VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20064)
*Bingao Chen, Lihan Zha, C. Karen Liu, Guy Tevet*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: We present FoveaVLA, a Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model that replaces the wrist camera with learned foveation actions. Wrist cameras provide close-up detail but restrict the view to the end-effector's surroundings. External cameras see the whole scene, but the policy receives them at low resolution, so the fine detail needed for manipulation is lost. FoveaVLA recovers this detail by predicting, alongside each action chunk, the interaction region to observe at the next policy query. The policy then receives crops of these regions, taken from the original-resolution images, together with the full views. We build FoveaVLA on $π_{0.5}$ in two configurations, Single and Dual, using one or two external stereo cameras with full and foveal RGB and depth inputs, and post-train both on DROID with RoboInter interaction annotations. Across four real-world task suites, FoveaVLA-Single achieves 70.8% success with one external RGB-D view, and FoveaVLA-Dual achieves 77.1%, versus 52.1% for the wrist-equipped $π_{0.5}$. Both match the baseline on plain pick-and-place and improve success with distractors, cloth manipulation, and far-away objects.


### [486] [Investigating Behavior Cloning from Few Demonstrations for Autonomous Driving based on Bird's-Eye View in Simulated Cities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20203)
*Eric Aislan Antonelo, Gustavo Claudio Karl Couto, Christian Möller, Pedro Henrique Fernandes*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: This paper investigates the use of Behavior Cloning (BC) for autonomous driving from a bird's-eye view (BEV) perspective in simulated urban environments. BC uses supervised learning to mimic expert driving behaviors. Previous works have applied BC in the CARLA simulator but did not fully address the challenges of traffic light compliance. Our approach enhances BC by integrating a kernel density estimator to adjust training sample weights based on action density, thereby improving the learning of rare but critical actions such as stopping at red lights and accelerating at green lights, specially in scenarios of scarce number of expert demonstrations. Using BEV inputs, which provide an abstract top-down view of the driving environment, our method simplifies the policy learning process. The trained convolutional neural network (CNN) outputs steering and acceleration actions based on these BEV inputs and additional state variables. Experimental results in the CARLA simulator demonstrate that our weighted BC method significantly improves driving performance, achieving higher route completion compared to standard BC. This weighted approach proved to be crucial in learning correct driving behaviors, particularly in test environments not encountered during training, highlighting its potential for enhancing autonomous vehicle navigation.


### [487] [Efficient Test-Time Adaptation For Robot Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20318)
*Motasem Alfarra, Pietro Mazzaglia, Markus Peschl, Daniel Dijkman, Christos Louizos*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Reinforcement-learning policies for robot locomotion can fail at deployment when operating conditions shift (e.g., rough or slippery terrain, payload changes, sensing degradation), and retraining or manual recalibration is often infeasible under on-device latency and memory constraints. We study test-time adaptation (TTA) for robotic control: adapting a pretrained policy online and in a self-supervised manner during execution, without access to rewards. Our starting point is a lightweight baseline that updates only the actor using a frozen pretrained critic as a deployment-time performance surrogate. Building on this, we introduce TEMPO, a test-time entropy-regularized, memory-guided policy optimization method that stabilizes critic-driven updates under severe shift. TEMPO combines (i) an entropy-promoting regularizer on the critic's predictive distribution to mitigate overconfident extrapolation, and (ii) a score-based memory, inspired by prioritized replay, that prioritizes low-value ("hard") observations and applies importance sampling to focus limited computation on the most informative deployment conditions. Across Go1, Go2, and G1 in MuJoCo Playground under four representative shifts and multiple architectures, TEMPO yields consistent gains, reaching up to 80% improvement in a single-device setting. We further validate on a physical Unitree Go2 with an added calf payload, where fully on-device adaptation improves performance over the non-adapted baseline across real-world episodes.


### [488] [Modeling and Understanding Neural Weights of Humanoid Policies with Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20606)
*Huixuan Cheng, Bike Zhang, Shankar Sastry, Koushil Sreenath*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Recent advances in robot learning have driven rapid progress in humanoid control, enabling increasingly agile behaviors in the real world. However, humanoid control networks are typically obtained through conventional training procedures, while comparatively little attention has been given to directly modeling the distribution of their learned weights and generating functional networks in weight space. In this work, we investigate whether high-performing humanoid control networks can be generatively modeled directly through their weights. We adopt a latent diffusion framework that couples a linear autoencoder with a Diffusion Transformer. The autoencoder compresses actor network parameters into a compact latent space, and the Diffusion Transformer models the distribution of these latents. Trained on networks collected from reinforcement learning for the Unitree G1 robot, our approach generates deployable networks that perform various motions in the real world, including walking, dancing, and spin kick. Beyond generation, we further study the learned weight space to better understand the structure captured by these humanoid control networks. We identify a structural principle in the first-layer weights that consistently reflects the robot's kinematic prior. Notably, this kinematic prior emerges under both Reinforcement Learning (RL) and Imitation Learning (IL) training, suggesting that it is not specific to a particular optimization process but may instead reflect common knowledge associated with humanoid embodiment. Together, these results demonstrate the feasibility of generative modeling in humanoid network weight space, while providing initial insights into the information encoded by their learned weights. Additional results are available on our project website: this https URL


### [489] [Hierarchical Residual Policy Learning for Real-World Mobile Manipulation with Sparse Human Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20607)
*Zhefei Gong, Hefeifei Jiang, Zehao Wang, Jiachen Li*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Mobile Manipulation (MoMa) is a cornerstone of autonomous robotic systems, yet real-world fine-tuning remains a formidable challenge because of high-dimensional action spaces and intricate coupling between heterogeneous modules. In this paper, we propose HiRe-MoMa (Hierarchical Residual Mobile Manipulation), a systematic framework for stable and sample-efficient real-world fine-tuning of complex MoMa systems. At its core, HiRe-MoMa adopts a Hierarchical Residual Architecture that fine-tunes a frozen base policy. A Policy-Agnostic Residual learns additive corrections atop the base policy, while Hierarchical Decoupling splits the residual into mobile-base and manipulator-arm heads with unidirectional conditioning, mitigating gradient interference and aligning corrections with the platform's physical hierarchy. To improve sample efficiency in data-expensive real-world settings, we propose Expert-Guided Value Alignment, which leverages sparse human interventions with under 20% training coverage to reshape the critic's value landscape, effectively guiding autonomous exploration through expert priors. Extensive real-world experiments on the TIAGo++ platform demonstrate that HiRe-MoMa consistently enhances task performance with only around 30 minutes of online interaction, regardless of the base policy architecture. To our knowledge, this is among the first frameworks to enable efficient on-robot fine-tuning for mobile manipulation in the real world, providing a practical paradigm for deploying complex robotic systems in dynamic, human-centric environments. Further details can be found on our project website: this https URL


### [490] [Human Demonstrations as Post-Training Data for Any Robot Body: Retargeting the Object, Not the Hand](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20351)
*Nagasrikanth Kallakuri*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Human demonstrations are today used largely for pretraining vision-language-action models, with little ability to use the same data for target-embodiment or task-oriented training, which scalable deployment of robots in industrial and general environments depends on. A human demonstration recorded on a simple and scalable device such as a mobile phone can serve as post-training data for a robot whose body differs in structure and capability from the human who recorded it. This is made possible not by retargeting the hand's motion but the object's. A capture yields a body-free record: the object's pose over time, its contact intervals, and a reconstructed 3D object with provenance. Any robot, given as a URDF and a short descriptor, is solved against that record and scored in simulation against the demonstrated object motion as ground truth: the human-to-simulation leg of human-to-simulation-to-robot transfer, measured per robot. Each (robot, episode) pair gets one outcome: pass, failure with a named mode, or refusal, with its reason, before any motion. The method runs as a working system over a growing collection; the set analysed here is a sample of it: 42 captures solved on 8 robots, 157 pairs, of which 31.2% pass, 52.2% fail, 14.0% are refused and 2.5% are unauditable (kinematic feasibility, +/-50 mm / +/-15 deg, in simulation). Refusals held up: every pair the checks refuse on stored evidence yet ran anyway failed (13 of 13; 5 on a body whose public model welds its hand). A description file could not be shown to screen a robot: its outcomes were barely more predictable than guessing. No policy has yet been trained on the record. The record decides what a robot may post-train on; whether the policy then works on the real robot is the simulation-to-robot question, answered downstream.


### [491] [MorphAct: Robot Policies Morphing with the Worlds They Act In](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20019)
*Ziyan Liu, Jiting Liu, Weihong Zhong, Wenda Yu, Yeqiu Chen, Jason Li, Xiaojiang Peng, Shuyuan Hu, Kai Wang, Bo Zhao*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Progress toward general intelligence in the physical world requires scaling robot learning across diverse environments and interactions. However, existing robot policies use shared parameters that remain fixed across interaction contexts. When different operations favor competing parameter adjustments, fitting their mixture can leave individual operations insufficiently adapted. Our atomic-task diagnostics reveal competing update directions in both vision-language and action parameters, with source-improving updates increasing loss on other tasks. This motivates MorphAct, a contextual parameterization framework that dynamically adjusts robot policy parameters as interaction contexts change. Two coupled generators produce context-dependent low-rank updates for the vision-language and action modules. Their coupling follows the policy's computational dependency: vision-language updates shape the representations that condition action-side parameter generation. Both generators are trained jointly through the native action objective, without operation labels or expert-adapter targets. MorphAct improves performance across three VLA backbones on LIBERO and Meta-World, with gains of up to 16.30%. In real-world evaluation, it achieves gains of up to 14% over $π_{0.5}$. Ablations support the benefits of contextual parameter generation and representation coupling.


### [492] [MUCO: A Mani-Unit-Structured Manipulation Framework for Compositional Long-Horizon Robot Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19913)
*Xiangyi Wei, Hanzhe Yao, Jingwen Shang, Zhenhong Wu, Yang Li, Changbo Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Learning and executing long-horizon robot manipulation as complete task trajectories raises data-collection costs, lim?its capability reuse, and causes local failures to cascade into later task stages. We introduce manipulation units (MUs) as reusable operations, each defined by a local goal, an initi?ation condition, and observable success and failure criteria. MUs are learned and verified individually. During execution, consecutive MUs remain coupled through shared environ?ment dynamics. We formulate MU-Structured Manipulation (MUSM) and realize it through MU-Centric Orchestration (MUCO). Given language and vision, MUCO constructs an MU sequence, matches each MU to compatible policy models in a frozen capability registry, and dispatches the model with the highest calibrated success rate. Visual verification checks MU outcomes against success and failure criteria and governs transitions, enabling retry or fallback to another model from the current state without re-executing verified MUs. Canonical keys enable checkpoint reuse for equivalent MUs across tasks without new demonstrations or updates. We evaluate MUCO on RLBench, RoboTwin 2.0, and five real-world tasks. MUCO achieves higher complete-task success and verified progress, stronger failure containment, and lower error propagation than baselines. Results demonstrate cross-task MU reuse and het?erogeneous policy composition without joint training, shared backbones, or common action representations.

### [493] [Physics-Guided Multi-Fidelity Surrogate Modeling for Natural Supercavity Geometry of an Axisymmetric Conical Cavitator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18886)
*Vinh Pham, Van Tu Nguyen, Warn-Gyu Park*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

Abstract: Natural supercavitation behind conical cavitators generates a vapor-filled envelope that, when sustained over the full body of a high-speed underwater vehicles, reduces hydrodynamic skin-friction drag and enables performance levels unattainable in fully wetted flow. Accurate prediction of maximum cavity length and maximum cavity radius and local cavity profile as functions of cavitation number and cone half-angle is essential for both the static design and the real-time shape management of super-cavitating torpedo cavitators. Classical analytical models provide computationally inexpensive predictions but exhibit systematic disagreements in the practically important regime of moderate cavitation number and large angle, where slender-body assumptions begin to break down. Existing data-driven machine learning approaches offer higher accuracy but are constrained by the prohibitive cost of multiphase computational fluid dynamics (CFD) sweeps over the full design space. This study proposes a physics-guided multi-fidelity surrogate framework based on Extended Hierarchical Kriging (PG-EHK) that addresses this challenge by fusing the multiple competing analytical cavity formulas as non-level low-fidelity (LF) trend functions corrected by a sparse set of high-fidelity (HF) CFD data. It simultaneously incorporates the analytical models regardless of their local fidelity ordering, using Bayesian-estimated scaling factors to construct the trend function as physical baseline. The resulting surrogate maps operational condition inputs to cavity geometry responses using fewer than 20 high-fidelity CFD evaluations. Cross-validation on five independent hold-out CFD cases demonstrates that the proposed PG-EHK surrogate achieves 61% reduction in prediction error compared to the best-performing individual analytical approach.

### [494] [Extreme-Scale Super-Resolution of Homogeneous Turbulence from a Single Realization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20305)
*Arth Sojitra, Muhammad Abid, Omer San*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

Abstract: Homogeneous turbulent flows exhibit recurring multiscale structures across spatially separated regions, suggesting that a single flow realization may contain sufficient information to learn representations of unresolved scales. We investigate this hypothesis through an extreme super-resolution framework in which a wide-activation deep super-resolution (WDSR) network is trained exclusively on overlapping patches extracted from a single high-resolution turbulent realization. Previously unseen flow fields are reconstructed through patchwise prediction followed by Hann-window-weighted overlap-add assembly. The framework is evaluated for two-dimensional homogeneous isotropic turbulence at super-resolution factors of x32 and x64, representing severe spatial under-resolution. Despite the limited training data, the proposed approach reconstructs coherent vortical structures and recovers fine-scale features more accurately than conventional interpolation. Spectral and statistical analyses further demonstrate improved recovery of high-wavenumber energy content and multiscale flow characteristics. To assess the effectiveness of exploiting internal spatial information, we compare the proposed framework against WDSR models trained on 1, 25, 50, and 100 complete high-resolution snapshots without patch extraction. This comparison distinguishes the benefits of localized learning from those associated with increasing the number of training realizations. The results demonstrate that exploiting spatial redundancy within a single turbulent realization enables accurate reconstruction of previously unseen flow fields, even under extreme spatial under-resolution. These findings establish single-realization patch-based learning as a promising data-efficient approach for turbulent super-resolution when high-resolution training data are scarce.

### [495] [Non-Abelian quantum Hall states at strong Landau level mixing: finding anyons with first-principles AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18967)
*Pierre-Antoine Graham, Ahmed Abouelkomsan, Liang Fu*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

Abstract: Non-Abelian anyons, strange particles forming the basis of fault-tolerant topological quantum computing, are predicted to emerge in fractional quantum Hall states at even-denominator filling factors. Identifying which topological order is realized has proven to be a challenging problem that requires an accurate, non-perturbative treatment of realistic Landau level mixing, which has so far been missing from theoretical studies. Here we present the first complete solution of non-Abelian quantum Hall problem that includes all electrons and all Landau levels, for spin-polarized electrons at filling factor $\nu = 3/2$. We develop a momentum-resolved neural-network variational wave function, optimized solely on the microscopic Hamiltonian, which gives direct access to the topologically degenerate ground states. Without any physics input about the expected outcome, it discovers a degenerate ground-state manifold whose entanglement reveals anyons with quantum dimension $\sqrt{2}$, the hallmark of Ising-type non-Abelian order. By directly computing the overlap with trial wave functions, we further show that Landau level mixing favors the anti-Pfaffian over other candidate states. Our results demonstrate the power of neural-network wave functions in finding and characterizing non-Abelian anyons from first principles, in regimes of strong Landau level mixing, relevant to many recent experiments.
